## Extended FX arsenal

| Feature | Description | Core | CLI/MCP | GUI | Status | Research | Plan |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ping Pong Delay | A hand-built two-delay-line network (not the plain Tone.PingPongDelay built-in, which hardwires 100% cross-feedback with no dial) as a per-track insert — pingPongTime/Feedback/Mix plus continuously-variable pingPongCrossFeed and delay-time LFO wobble (pingPongWobbleRate/Depth, research 21 row 4) rather than a binary ping-pong toggle. | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ Done | [`17-track-fx-arsenal.md`](research/17-track-fx-arsenal.md) | [`phase-22-stream-ac.md`](phase-22-stream-ac.md) |
| Beat Repeat | Grid/gate/chance/mode stutter-repeat — scheduling-layer note/hit re-triggering in engine.ts's tick() (not a Tone.js audio node, per research 17 §4.3), with a per-note-position-seeded RNG for the chance roll (research 21 row 5) so re-renders are bit-for-bit reproducible. | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ Done | [`17-track-fx-arsenal.md`](research/17-track-fx-arsenal.md) | [`phase-22-stream-ac.md`](phase-22-stream-ac.md) |
| Expose Chorus-Ensemble / Phaser-Flanger as a per-track insert | Retired the old shared, un-configurable chorusBus/phaserBus/sendMod mod-send machinery; chorusMode (off/chorus/ensemble/vibrato)/chorusRate/Depth/Mix and phaserRate/Depth/Mix are now real per-track inserts, same one-instance-per-track precedent as EQ3/compressor. | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ Done | [`17-track-fx-arsenal.md`](research/17-track-fx-arsenal.md) | [`phase-22-stream-ac.md`](phase-22-stream-ac.md) |
| Saturator | Tone.WaveShaper-based character saturation with an analog/warm/clip/fold curve family (authored once per curve CHANGE, not per sample) and a drive-controlled pre-gain into the shaper. | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ Done | [`17-track-fx-arsenal.md`](research/17-track-fx-arsenal.md) | [`phase-22-stream-ac.md`](phase-22-stream-ac.md) |
| 7-band parametric EQ | HP/LP (selectable slope 12/24/48/96 dB/oct + Q) + 2 shelf bands + 3 parametric bell bands (freq/gain/Q each), each of the 7 independently enabled via its own on flag (no shared "neutral value = off" trick — HP/LP have no true no-op frequency). New EffectType 'eq7', additive to Phase 22 Stream AA's reorderable per-track chain; built entirely on Tone.Filter (covers both the HP/LP rolloff-cascade half and the native peaking/lowshelf/highshelf types research 17 flagged as two separate halves). | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ Done | [`17-track-fx-arsenal.md`](research/17-track-fx-arsenal.md) | [`phase-23-stream-bd.md`](phase-23-stream-bd.md) |
| Auto Filter / Auto Pan / Tremolo | Dedicated Ableton-named devices — thin wrappers around Tone.AutoFilter/AutoPanner/Tremolo, ADDITIVE entries in the same reorderable effect chain (autoFilter/autoPan/tremolo EffectType members), each with its own Rate/Depth/Mix (Tremolo also Spread). The shared LFO destination matrix already covers the sonic capability; the value here is Ableton-authentic naming and a third, independent modulation source, not new sound. | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ Done | [`17-track-fx-arsenal.md`](research/17-track-fx-arsenal.md) | [`phase-23-stream-be.md`](phase-23-stream-be.md) |
| Redux (downsampling half) | A new bitcrushRate field on the EXISTING bitcrush type (not a new EffectType) — Ableton's own Redux is one device, two dimensions, and bit-reduction already owned bitcrush's bit-depth half. A hand-built sample-and-hold decimator (raw ScriptProcessorNode; Tone.js has no built-in Rate/Jitter node), gated by the SAME bitcrushMix as bit-depth reduction — one shared dry/wet knob for the whole device. | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ Done | [`17-track-fx-arsenal.md`](research/17-track-fx-arsenal.md) | [`phase-23-stream-be.md`](phase-23-stream-be.md) |
| Utility (stereo width / gain trim) | Near-free via Tone.StereoWidener (utilityWidth, 0=mono/1=max stereo/0.5=neutral default) plus a static utilityGain dB trim — a mixing-hygiene tool, not a sound-design reach. No Mix field (like eq3, the chain's per-instance bypass is its only "off"). | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ Done | [`17-track-fx-arsenal.md`](research/17-track-fx-arsenal.md) | [`phase-23-stream-be.md`](phase-23-stream-be.md) |
| Grain Delay | A hand-built granular pitch-shifting delay — Tone.Delay + Tone.Gain feedback + Tone.PitchShift (Tone.js's own internal granular pitch-shift algorithm, exposing a real windowSize grain-size control) in one feedback loop, so every repeat is both re-granulated and re-pitched (cumulative shimmer). A real EffectType chain member (unlike Stream AC's fixed inserts), synth tracks only. | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ Done | [`17-track-fx-arsenal.md`](research/17-track-fx-arsenal.md) | [`phase-23-stream-bf.md`](phase-23-stream-bf.md) |
| Vinyl Distortion | Tone.WaveShaper asymmetric tape/record-style soft-clip saturation + a seeded, reproducible surface-noise/crackle bed (a hand-generated buffer via a streaming mulberry32 PRNG, not Tone.Noise — which has no public seed API and would make renders non-reproducible) plus a tone-tilt filter. | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ Done | [`17-track-fx-arsenal.md`](research/17-track-fx-arsenal.md) | [`phase-23-stream-bf.md`](phase-23-stream-bf.md) |
| Resonators | A bank of 5 tuned Tone.Filter bandpass nodes (fifths/major/minor/octaves/harmonic interval sets around a root frequency, Q as the ring/decay proxy) approximating physical resonance — the closest a plain biquad filter bank gets without Corpus's AudioWorklet-tier custom DSP. | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ Done | [`17-track-fx-arsenal.md`](research/17-track-fx-arsenal.md) | [`phase-23-stream-bf.md`](phase-23-stream-bf.md) |
| Corpus | Resonant-body physical-modeling effect — no Tone.js primitive gets close; AudioWorklet-tier custom DSP, lowest priority. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`17-track-fx-arsenal.md`](research/17-track-fx-arsenal.md) | — |
| Gate | dotbeat's only "sidechain" concept today is `duckSource`/`duckAmount` (document.ts:299-300) — a scheduled volume dip synced to a source track's kick-lane hits, not a real audio-triggered envelope follower (engine.ts:3389-3402: "not an audio-analysis sidechain... dips this track's volume whenever duckSource's kick lane has a hit at this step"). Ableton's Gate (manual ch.28 pp.573-575) is a full Threshold/Return/Attack/Hold/Release/Floor noise gate with a Flip (duck) mode and full EQ'd external sidechain — independently the sharpest single finding of two research passes (48 then 67). New `EffectType: 'gate'` built from `Tone.Follower` driving a `Tone.Gain` via threshold comparison, same pattern the codebase already uses for `duckAmount`'s scheduled envelope but continuous/audio-rate. Self-sidechain-only v1 (real audio-triggered cross-track sidechain is a stretch goal, not required — already closes most of the everyday-use gap). | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`67-ableton-vs-dotbeat-audio-effect-reference.md`](research/67-ableton-vs-dotbeat-audio-effect-reference.md) | — |
| Plain tempo-synced Delay insert | dotbeat only has the two specialized delays (Grain Delay, Ping Pong Delay) — no plain synced delay insert exists, distinct from the shared reverb/delay send buses. Ableton's base Delay device: plain tempo-synced/free stereo delay, per-line filter, LFO on time+filter, 3 time-change smoothing modes (manual ch.28 pp.556-559). New `EffectType: 'delay'` using `Tone.FeedbackDelay` (already proven for the shared `delayBus`) wired as a genuine per-track insert — own node instance per track, `delayTime`/`delayFeedback`/`delayFilterFreq`/`delaySync` fields, `delayTime` expressible as a tempo-synced division reusing the `LFO_SYNC_RATES` enum pattern. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`67-ableton-vs-dotbeat-audio-effect-reference.md`](research/67-ableton-vs-dotbeat-audio-effect-reference.md) | — |
| Real per-track Reverb insert (replacing the single shared hardcoded bus) | dotbeat's reverb is `Tone.Reverb({decay:2.2, wet:1})` (engine.ts:1768) — one hardcoded shared instance, decay only, no per-track insert, no IR import. Ableton's real Reverb/Hybrid Reverb: input filter, early reflections with Spin, full diffusion network, internal chorus, Freeze/Flat/Cut, optional convolution IR import (manual ch.28 pp.608-611, 580-586). New `EffectType: 'reverb'` using `Tone.Reverb`'s existing `decay`/`preDelay` params plus a new `reverbColor` (simple input-filter tilt) — closes most of the "shallow reverb" gap without chasing Hybrid Reverb's convolution-IR complexity. dotbeat's current shared reverb is real and usable, just shallow — sequence behind Gate/Delay/Knee, which close more everyday gaps per build-hour. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`67-ableton-vs-dotbeat-audio-effect-reference.md`](research/67-ableton-vs-dotbeat-audio-effect-reference.md) | — |
| Bass Mono on the existing Utility insert | dotbeat's Utility (Tone.StereoWidener + Tone.Volume, `utilityWidth`/`utilityGain`) covers 2 of Ableton's 9 Utility controls. Ableton's real device also has Bass Mono with its own Audition solo-the-lows toggle, Mid/Side mode, per-channel phase invert, Channel Mode, Mono switch, Balance, Mute, and a DC offset filter (manual ch.28 p.645 screenshot). Bass Mono is the cheapest genuinely-new-DSP item: `Tone.Filter` lowpass split path below a `utilityBassMonoFreq` cutoff, summed to mono via a `Tone.Gain`-based mid-channel merge, recombined with the already-widened highs. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`67-ableton-vs-dotbeat-audio-effect-reference.md`](research/67-ableton-vs-dotbeat-audio-effect-reference.md) | — |
| Ring modulation (Shifter's ring-mod mode, as its own standalone effect) | Ableton's Shifter unifies pitch-shift/frequency-shift/ring-modulation in one device (manual ch.28 pp.624-628); dotbeat has no true multiplicative ring mod anywhere (autoFilter/tremolo/phaser don't cover this territory). New `EffectType: 'ringMod'`, named standalone rather than folded into a 3-in-1 Shifter design to match dotbeat's existing one-effect-one-job convention (resonator/grainDelay are already split out rather than merged). `Tone.Gain` whose `.gain` is driven by an audio-rate `Tone.Oscillator` — true multiplicative ring mod, no dedicated Tone.js class needed. Fields: `ringModFreq` (Hz, 1-5000), `ringModMix`. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`67-ableton-vs-dotbeat-audio-effect-reference.md`](research/67-ableton-vs-dotbeat-audio-effect-reference.md) | — |
| EQ7 Adaptive Q + Stereo/L-R/M-S processing modes | dotbeat's shipped 7-band EQ (eq7) is a fixed HP+LoShelf+3×Bell+HiShelf+LP topology, mono-summed, with static Q fields. Ableton's EQ Eight additionally offers Adaptive Q (Q auto-increases with boost/cut amount for a more analog-consistent curve) and Stereo/L-R/M-S processing modes, plus 2× oversampling and an Audition (solo-a-band) mode (manual ch.28 p.568 screenshot). Adaptive Q needs per-band gain-to-Q coupling logic in the live audio-param update path; Stereo/L-R/M-S needs per-channel-pair filter routing, a bigger lift (splitting the signal path, not just a coefficient) — defer until eq7 usage in practice shows the mono-linked behavior is a real complaint. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`67-ableton-vs-dotbeat-audio-effect-reference.md`](research/67-ableton-vs-dotbeat-audio-effect-reference.md) | — |
| Auto Filter envelope-follower section (alongside its existing LFO) | dotbeat's `autoFilter*` fields are LFO-only today. Ableton's Auto Filter has a full envelope-follower section (Attack/Hold/Release, S&H quantize, sidechain input) driving the filter in addition to the LFO; Auto Pan-Tremolo's merged UI also has a Shape control reshaping the LFO waveform continuously between ramp/sine/square, which dotbeat's `tremoloRate/Depth/Spread/Mix` has no equivalent of (manual ch.28 pp.519,526). Adding `Tone.Follower`-driven modulation of `autoFilterBaseFrequency` reuses the same `Tone.Follower` primitive the Gate effect (above) would introduce — sequence Gate first and share the envelope-follower code path. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`67-ableton-vs-dotbeat-audio-effect-reference.md`](research/67-ableton-vs-dotbeat-audio-effect-reference.md) | — |

## Automation

| Feature | Description | Core | CLI/MCP | GUI | Status | Research | Plan |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Per-track picker + draggable curve | Pick a track/param, draw breakpoints, playback verified to follow the drawn curve exactly. | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ Done | — | [`phase-20-automation-lanes.md`](phase-20-automation-lanes.md) |
| Curved automation segments + exact numeric breakpoint entry | Phase 26 Stream DI shipped this: `BeatAutomationPoint.interpolation?: 'linear' | 'hold' | 'curve'` (document.ts), canonically elided when 'linear' (a trailing `interpolation=hold|curve` token, same discipline as v0.10's note fields — `pointOptionalTokens`/`parsePointOptionalFields` in serialize.ts/parse.ts). Lives on the segment's START point, mirroring Ableton's own per-segment gesture. GUI (`AutomationLane`, ArrangementView.tsx): Alt/Option-drag a segment bows it (a live quadratic-bezier-toward-the-drag-point preview, committing a `curve` flag on release — the committed render/engine both use a fixed quadratic ease-in, `curveEase`); a per-point right-click popup offers a linear/hold/curve toggle AND an exact numeric `<input>` (both write through the same `set` op); the live drag-value label (`drag.value`, previously computed but never rendered) now shows in a floating tag while dragging. Engine (`interpolateAutomation`, engine.ts): branches on the flag — 'hold' steps instantly to the next point's value at its time instead of ramping, 'curve' eases via `curveEase` before the linear/log blend — a real playback difference, verified with actual recorded/measured audio (not just internal state) in `ui/verify-phase26-stream-di.mjs`. CLI/MCP: `beat automate --interpolation linear|hold|curve` / `beat_automate`'s `interpolation` arg. | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ Done | [`65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md`](research/65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md) | [`phase-26-plan.md`](phase-26-plan.md) |
| Same-row curve overlay | Draw the automation curve directly over the clip row instead of only in a dedicated sub-lane. DEFERRED by Phase 41 Stream C, which shipped the other four Automation GUI rows: unlike those, this one is not automation code at all — the drawing surface is the clip BLOCK inside ArrangementView.tsx's TrackRow, so it belongs to whoever owns the arrangement grid, not to the automation lane (now its own module, ui/src/components/AutomationLane.tsx). Trigger to build: schedule it with an arrangement-area stream, and reuse AutomationLane's exported valueToY/segValue path rather than writing a second curve renderer — note that path is now LOG-scaled for cutoff to match the engine, so a naive linear overlay would disagree with both the sub-lane and playback. | — | — | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`50-ableton-vs-dotbeat-live-concepts.md`](research/50-ableton-vs-dotbeat-live-concepts.md) | — |
| Batched /automate write (one gesture = one undo entry) | Found by Phase 41 Stream C while building Draw Mode paint and shape insertion. The daemon's POST /automate is strictly per-point (op set/remove, one breakpoint each) and passes no `coalesceKey` to writeIfChanged, so a gesture that writes N breakpoints costs N daemon round-trips, N file writes and — the part that is actually felt — N separate undo entries: one painted sweep currently needs ~10-20 Ctrl+Z to fully undo. Stream C did not fix it because src/daemon/daemon.ts was another stream's file that night, and worked around it instead by reducing every run before committing (simplifyAutomationPoints) so N is 10-20 rather than 64. Fix: an additive `op: 'run'` on /automate taking {points: [{time,value}], replaceSpan: [from,to]} and applying it through the same core primitives in one writeIfChanged with a coalesceKey — the GUI side is already funnelled through one writeRun() helper in AutomationLane.tsx, so it is a single call site to switch. Cheap and well-scoped; sequence with any daemon-area stream. | — | — | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | — | — |
| Automation manual-override suspends (not erases) + Re-Enable Automation | Ableton: nudging an automated control while not recording silently "overrides" it (LED off, plays your manual value); a Control Bar button snaps every overridden control back to what's written (manual ch.25 pp.484-485). No mechanism to trigger this exists yet — dotbeat has no live-record-while-playing automation entry point. Parked as a design note until live automation recording exists; when it does, mirror Ableton's semantic exactly rather than silently overwriting drawn automation on first touch. dotbeat's structurally different safety net (undo/redo, checkpoint/history) already covers "try something, revert" at a coarser, arguably more useful grain in the meantime. | — | — | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md`](research/65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md) | — |
| Track/arrangement-scoped automation independent of any single clip (Lock Envelopes equivalent) | Automation is currently scoped to one clip at a time by explicit v0.9 design (document.ts:539-543: "deliberately NOT modeled at the live track / non-clip level") — the picker/curve target only a track's first-playing clip. Ableton's Automation Envelopes are keyed to Arrangement position (or the Session clip that recorded them), a genuinely separate mechanism from Clip Envelopes, with Lock Envelopes letting a curve stay pinned to song position even as clips underneath it move (manual ch.25 p.492, ch.3 p.57). The single biggest structural automation gap named by two independent chapters — needs its own design pass before building: attach automation at the scene/section slot-mapping level, not the clip object, rather than inventing a second parallel track-level data structure. Don't build shape-insertion/stretch-skew automation features against today's clip-only model without revisiting this first — every clip-scoping limitation (global-wipe Delete Automation, "first-playing clip only," absent loop-mode automation) traces back to this one root cause. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md`](research/65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md) | — |
| Log-scale y-axis | Shipped in Phase 41 Stream C, and it turned out to be a CORRECTNESS bug rather than the readability nicety this row described. ui/src/audio/engine.ts calls interpolateAutomation(points, step, log) with log=true at exactly one call site — cutoff, "frequency perception is logarithmic" — so between two cutoff breakpoints the engine plays a*(b/a)^t while the lane drew a straight line in LINEAR space: the picture disagreed with the sound. On a log axis the engine's own geometric interpolation IS a straight line, so the drawing becomes exact; eased "curve" segments now sample through a segValue() mirroring the same branch. Found by reading a pilot screenshot: a 20->4000 Hz sine inserted on the owner's twin-souls melody was squashed into the bottom 22% of a linear 20..18000 lane. Guarded to strictly-positive ranges (ln(0) has no answer) and to the params the engine actually treats logarithmically, with a note to grep the engine before extending the set. The same bug had a second half in the paint gesture, whose simplify tolerance was a fraction of the RAW range (180 Hz for cutoff) and so flattened the bottom of a painted arc while keeping every sample at the top; reduction now runs in normalized axis space. Verify C7 clicks the lane's midline and asserts the value written is the geometric mean (~600 Hz) rather than the linear midpoint (~9010 Hz). | — | — | ✅ done | ✅ Done | [`50-ableton-vs-dotbeat-live-concepts.md`](research/50-ableton-vs-dotbeat-live-concepts.md) | — |
| Unlinked clip-envelope loop length (independent of the clip's own tiling) | Ableton's longest, most novel clip-envelope section (manual ch.26 pp.503-506): a clip envelope's own local loop/region length, unlinked from the clip's sample loop — enabling a long shape over a short loop, a short rhythmic-gating shape over a long sample, or a hidden-grid unsynced LFO shape, all via one mechanism. `BeatAutomationLane` has no loop-range field at all — a drawn curve is always tiled to the clip's own `loopBars*16` period. Additive schema field mirroring the exact `BeatClipLoop | null` pattern already used twice: `loop: {start,end} | null`, absent = today's behavior. Engine change is localized to the tiling math already in the automation-read path. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`66-ableton-vs-dotbeat-clip-envelopes.md`](research/66-ableton-vs-dotbeat-clip-envelopes.md) | — |
| Cross-parameter automation copy/paste | Ableton: an envelope's copied data can be pasted onto a different parameter's lane, deliberately ungated by type compatibility (manual ch.25 p.492). dotbeat's `postAutomation` route only ever writes points into the lane the drag originated in — no copy/paste concept for automation exists at all. A keyboard shortcut (Cmd/Ctrl+C on a selected lane, Cmd/Ctrl+V on a target) that copies the source lane's points normalized to its own min/max and re-denormalizes into the target param's range on paste, writing each point via the existing `setAutomationPoint` primitive — deliberately don't gate by type compatibility, matching Ableton's own stance. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md`](research/65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md) | — |
| Automation discovery UI (badge for already-automated params in the picker) | Shipped in Phase 41 Stream C, scoped wider than this row proposed. A dot on the picker's own <option>s is nearly a no-op by itself: ArrangementView's visibleParamsFor already auto-opens a lane for every automated param on the PRIMARY clip and then subtracts those from the picker's `available` list, so an option carrying automation on the clip you are looking at is never in the list to be dotted. What is genuinely invisible — and what this row was after — is automation on the track's OTHER clips, i.e. the sections you are not currently looking at. So the picker scans the whole track: a bullet on any option that carries automation somewhere, plus a row of chips naming each automated param with its point count, titled with which clips hold it, each chip clicking through to show that lane. Guarded by verify C6. | — | — | ✅ done | ✅ Done | [`65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md`](research/65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md) | — |
| Segment-level selection and drag on automation lanes | Shipped in Phase 41 Stream C. Shift+drag within 6px of a segment selects the two points flanking it and moves BOTH by the same delta, so the segment slides without changing its slope or length; the selection renders as a thick white overlay on every tiled repeat and clears itself when either endpoint stops existing. Shift rather than this row's proposed "click near, not on, a point" proximity tier: the lane's plain click on empty space already means "add a breakpoint here", and a proximity tier would kill that gesture within 6px of any existing curve — exactly where people add points. Two bugs were found by DRIVING the gesture rather than by any assertion, both now guarded (verify C5): the value delta had to move into NORMALIZED lane space (an absolute delta on the log cutoff axis moved the two endpoints by 5x and 9.4x and destroyed the slope the gesture exists to preserve), and the time delta is snapped to the drag grid ONCE and added to both endpoints rather than rounding each result (which produced 0.2057 vs 0.2029 steps for a single gesture). | — | — | ✅ done | ✅ Done | [`65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md`](research/65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md) | — |
| Draw Mode paint-a-run gesture for automation | Shipped in Phase 41 Stream C. A pencil toggle on the lane header arms draw mode; a drag then paints a run of breakpoints, sampled once per 16th step of CLIP-LOCAL time (not per pixel, so resolution is zoom-independent) with the gap between pointermove events interpolated — a fast sweep otherwise lands as a handful of isolated samples with straight chords between them, visibly not what was drawn. On release the stroke goes through the new simplifyAutomationPoints (see the Simplify Envelope row) at 2% of the lane height, so a 4-bar sweep commits ~10-20 points rather than 64. DELIBERATE DEVIATION from this row's own recommendation: it argued against Ableton's mode toggle in favour of the chance lane's plain-drag-paints, but that does not transfer — the chance lane has no other meaning for a plain drag, while the automation lane's plain drag IS place-a-breakpoint-and-position-it, the most important gesture there. The remaining options were an invisible modifier or a visible toggle, and an invisible modifier fails the only test that matters (someone opening the app and trying to draw a filter sweep). Guarded by ui/verify-phase41-stream-c.mjs C3/C4: a painted arc is reproduced rather than flattened to its endpoints, and points outside the painted span survive untouched. | — | — | ✅ done | ✅ Done | [`65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md`](research/65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md) | — |
| Simplify Envelope (geometric breakpoint-count reduction) | Core shipped in Phase 41 Stream C as src/core/automation-simplify.ts — this row's own "build sooner if manual curve-drawing starts producing visibly noisy diffs" trigger fired immediately, because Draw Mode paint samples once per 16th step and a 4-bar stroke is 64 breakpoints of mostly hand jitter. simplifyAutomationPoints is a pure Douglas-Peucker measured by VERTICAL deviation rather than perpendicular distance (perpendicular would mix 16th steps against Hz/dB/0..1, so its tolerance would mean something different for every param and every zoom level); points carrying an authored hold/curve flag are anchors and the two endpoints always survive, so it only ever deletes information it could recover, and survivors are the original objects. 10 known-answer tests in test/automate-simplify.test.ts, including the real workload: 64 samples of a cutoff sine reduce below 24 points with every original time re-read off the REDUCED polyline within tolerance. STILL DEFERRED, with a trigger: there is no `beat automate-simplify`, no MCP tool and no GUI button — today the only caller is the paint gesture, which reduces its own stroke before committing. Un-defer when a SECOND caller appears (live automation recording, or an imported/converted envelope), since a one-caller operation does not yet need the CLI+MCP+parity-row treatment a two-surface operation does. | ✅ done | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | undefined | [`65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md`](research/65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md) | — |
| Predefined automation shapes (sine/triangle/ADSR insertion) | Core + CLI shipped in Phase 37 Stream RC (`beat automate-shape`, src/core/automation-shape.ts); the GUI half shipped in Phase 41 Stream C, which is why this row read gui: missing while a finished generator sat behind the CLI. A wave-glyph button on the lane header opens a panel with shape (ramp/sine/triangle/exp/adsr), from/to, cycles and point count, spanning the clip's own tiling period. The geometry is NOT re-implemented in the GUI: ui/src/components/AutomationLane.tsx imports automationShapePoints straight from src/core (both automation helpers are pure and import nothing, so nothing Node-only reaches the bundle), so a sine inserted from the panel and one inserted by the CLI are the same points — asserted value-by-value against the core sampler in ui/verify-phase41-stream-c.mjs C1, with C2 guarding that a second insert REPLACES the lane rather than stacking on it. Defaults are the param's min up to the value the patch already sits at, so the inserted sweep arrives at the sound the track currently makes rather than at an arbitrary extreme. | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ Done | [`65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md`](research/65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md) | — |
| Stretch/skew a time-selected automation range | Ableton: four corner + four edge-midpoint drag handles on a hovered time selection do vertical stretch (rescale value range), horizontal stretch (time-rescale), and corner-drag skew, with a live rectangle overlay (manual ch.25 pp.489-490). Depends on a lane-local time-selection concept dotbeat doesn't have today (the existing `/selection` protocol is track/bar-range at the arrangement level, not lane-local) — build that first, then layer stretch/skew handles on top; skew is directly analogous to segment drag generalized to N points. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md`](research/65-ableton-vs-dotbeat-automation-envelopes.md) | — |

## Known usability gaps (backlog)

| Feature | Description | Core | CLI/MCP | GUI | Status | Research | Plan |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Automating a loop-mode project takes three hops before anything is possible | Measured in Phase 41 Stream C's GUI pilot, opening the owner's real ~/Documents/dotbeat/songs/twin-souls-study project cold. Automation is clip-scoped and song-mode-only by v0.9 design, so on a project with notes but no song block the arrangement's `A` toggle is disabled and NOTHING about automation is reachable. The app does guide you, but in three separate places and only one step at a time: the disabled button's tooltip ("add this track to a scene to automate its clip"), then the clip panel's hint, then "Place in Arrangement", which toasts "Add a song section first (+ section)" — and only then does + section create the scene, place both tracks and enable automation. Each message is correct and none of them names the whole path. The owner's own framing of this project is that the automation IS the song, so this is the first wall a real session hits. Fix candidates (arrangement area, not automation): have "Place in Arrangement" create the section it needs instead of refusing, or state the full two-step path in the disabled toggle's tooltip. | — | — | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | — | — |
| `beat board` records one keypress as many decisions across several boards (HIGH — it corrupts the owner's taste data) | FOUND 2026-07-28 BY THE OWNER, on their own picking session, and it is worse than an annoyance because the artifact this UI exists to produce is a record of what the owner actually preferred. Running `beat board boards` over 14 undecided batches, a single intended selection wrote SEVEN decisions in 29 seconds — `arp-env` picked five times and `arp-figures-2` twice, every one of them "v5", interleaved (02:49:41, :44, :54, :57, 02:50:04, :07, :10). The owner's words: "I meant to only select the first one!" Two distinct defects are implied and both need confirming against `src/serve/review-server.ts` and its client: (a) the numeric pick key repeats — no debounce, or the keyup is not consumed — so one press fires repeatedly; and (b) the repeat SURVIVES the advance to the next board, so a held/bounced key decides a batch the owner has not heard yet. (b) is the serious one: it fabricates preference data for unheard audio, and `beat-decisions.jsonl` is append-only with no undo, so recovery meant hand-editing the log and deleting `decision.json` files. Note the blind sibling `beat rate` writes the taste-model training log and very likely shares the handler — if so, the same bug can silently poison `beat-scores.jsonl`, which is the project's single most valuable asset. CHECK THAT FIRST. Minimum fixes: debounce/consume the keypress; require a fresh keydown after a board advance; and give the picker an undo (or make a decision revisable in-session) so a misfire does not need a text editor. TRIGGER: none — this is live every time the owner picks, and it silently degrades the data the whole taste program is built on. | — | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | — | — |
| Arrangement track headers detach from their lanes when scrolled right (reference-track pilot, HIGH) | THE FINDING THAT MOST HURTS A LONG ARRANGEMENT, from the 2026-07-27 GUI pilot whose goal was "lay a 30-chop reference track across the arrangement, mute it, unmute it, hear an 8-bar section". `.arr-row` is sized to the VIEWPORT (1600px) rather than to the timeline (5396px at 240 bars), so its `position:sticky` track header gives out past scrollLeft 1336 — at max scroll the header sits at x=-2460, i.e. entirely off-screen. Screenshot evidence: clip blocks run all the way to x=0 with no track name and, critically, NO M/S/volume/pan controls. On a 240-bar song at any usable zoom, the mute button the whole "typically muted reference track" workflow depends on is simply not reachable. ui/src/styles.css:2165 + ArrangementView.tsx:916. Two more layout findings from the same pilot, same area: (a) track header rows CLIP their own content — roughly 80px of controls in a 56px ROW_H with justify-content:center and no overflow handling, so the FIRST track's name is sliced (its checkbox/swatch invisible, confirmed independently in the very first screenshot of the session) and every track's scissors button paints over the next track's titlebar, worst on audio tracks which add a 4th child (ArrangementView.tsx:3287, ROW_H at :138); (b) making a selection SHIFTS the whole arrangement down 39px (measured 199->238 and back), which made the pilot wrongly conclude ruler-drag-select was broken and made its second drag land on a different track than aimed at — a user drag-selecting twice in a row hits the wrong lane. NOT FIXED HERE: all three live in ArrangementView.tsx/styles.css, owned by a concurrent stream on the night they were found. | — | — | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | — | — |
| An all-silent render passes every pathology screen and prints `nan` into its prose (feedback --offline pilot, MEDIUM) | FOUND by the 2026-07-27 CLI pilot on `beat feedback --offline`. Rendering a project whose notes are placed in no scene — the exact state a hurried user is in mid-build — produced 11s of pure silence, and the report called it healthy: a section table of `-inf` LUFS and `nan` crest, an arrangement-flatness finding reading "no adjacent pair moves more than nan LU and the whole arrangement spans only nan LU (loudest \"quiet\" -inf LUFS)", and then `pathology screens: no pathologies detected — all screens pass`. The unplaced-track warning DID fire up front (`⚠ track 'lead' has 2 notes but is placed in no scene`), which is the good half; the bad half is that total silence is the most obvious defect a render can have and the defect suite calls it clean. Two things to fix, probably together: a silence screen (whole-file, above the existing section-located dead-air screen, which only fires within a section map), and `nan`/`-inf` never reaching prose — a metric that bottomed out at the log floor should print as such, the way profile JSON already writes -Infinity as null with a documented meaning. | A mistyped SUBCOMMAND dumps the whole ~95KB catalog; a mistyped FLAG gets a perfect error (feedback --offline pilot, MEDIUM) | FOUND by the 2026-07-27 CLI pilot on `beat feedback --offline`, as a direct contrast the pilot drew itself. `beat feeback <file>` prints `unknown command "feeback"` and then the ENTIRE usage for every command — ~95KB, burying the one line that matters and, for an agent caller, burning the context window on a typo. | beat score cannot rank `beat source gen` seed candidates — resolved Phase 40 VB | The natural generative-audio audition loop — same prompt, N seeds, rank the candidates, adopt the winner — cannot route through beat score/adopt, which require a beat-vary batch manifest that generation never produces. Building examples/recipe-song (2026-07-14), the snare had to be picked from its three seed candidates by out-of-band FFT metrics instead of the taste loop, and the two losing samples stay registered in the media block with no record of the audition outcome. Fix direction: let `beat source gen` emit N seed variants as a scoreable batch (manifest + optional stitched audition.wav, mirroring vary --audition), so generated sounds join the same score/suggest/adopt history as synth-param variants. | ✅ done | ✅ done | — | ✅ Done | — | — |
| Engine: osc-group edits on polyphonic tracks break render scheduling | Varying the osc group (osc2Level/osc2Detune/subLevel/unisonVoices/unisonWidth/wtPos) on a POLYPHONIC synth track (simultaneous note starts, i.e. chords) makes the engine throw "Start time must be strictly greater than previous start time" (Tone.js scheduling) on BOTH render paths — offline compute and live capture. Repro: `beat vary examples/taste-t1/seed-005.beat chords osc --count 2 --render` (also seed-007). Surfaced 2026-07-18 as the recurring "2 failed" in every taste-collect run. CLOSED 2026-07-18: root cause was NOT unison/osc2 voice scheduling — the osc vary group also includes noiseLevel, and chain.noise is the one persistent Tone source shared by every note on a synth track, retriggered once per chord note at the identical slot time — i.e. exactly the pilot-111 noise-layer bug, already fixed by a7ac2c6's SynthChain.lastNoiseStart monotonic guard (--spread sets EVERY group param, so every osc batch raised noiseLevel on the chords track). Verified by neutering the guard (exact throw reproduces on the recorded repro) and restoring it (seed-005 and seed-007 osc batches render clean on both paths); the rest of the bank (osc2/osc3/uniPairs/sub/fm) is per-note Tone.PolySynth voices — a fresh voice per trigger, even for identical simultaneous pitches, verified hot with noise off — so no second guard is needed. taste-collect's osc-on-poly skip is removed; poly tracks are back in the osc taste pool. | ✅ done | ✅ done | — | ✅ Done | — | — |

## Versioning / history

| Feature | Description | Core | CLI/MCP | GUI | Status | Research | Plan |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| git-backed checkpoints, history panel, pin/restore | Explicit checkpoint/history/pin/restore over git — not automatic, deliberately. | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ Done | — | [`phase-15-history-panel.md`](phase-15-history-panel.md) |
| Musical-language git-merge conflict narration | A `beat merge --explain` that narrates a merge conflict in the same phrasing D8 already uses for diffs ("both changed trk_bass.cutoff: 1200Hz vs 800Hz") instead of raw <<<<<<< markers. Reuses D8's DiffEntry machinery unchanged. Pairs naturally with a narrower, currently-uncovered sibling: Ableton's Merging Sets lets a user drag a whole Set (or unfold it like a folder) to cherry-pick one track/clip/device chain out of another project file (manual ch.5 pp.131-134) — dotbeat's equivalent, `beat import-track <source.beat> <track-id> --into <dest.beat> [--as <new-id>]`, is a well-defined text operation given both files share one grammar and stable slugs, more precise than Ableton's drag-target ambiguity. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | — | ⬜ Not started | [`52-ableton-vs-dotbeat-files-and-sets.md`](research/52-ableton-vs-dotbeat-files-and-sets.md) | — |
| `beat diff --since <ref>` + `--rollup` (the agent's morning read of an owner GUI session) | research/128 §2.3, the GUI→agent half of the owner-agent-GUI loop. `beat diff --since <ref> <file>` is sugar for "what changed since checkpoint X" — the file's saved state at <ref> vs the CURRENT on-disk state (uncommitted edits included), reusing the existing gitShow/semantic-diff plumbing. <ref> is a checkpoint ref (from `beat history`) OR a pin NAME (from `beat pins`) — pins are `pin/<slug>` git tags, resolved by a new history.showFileAt that tries the ref verbatim then as a pin tag. `--rollup` collapses the semantic diff for agent consumption: net before→after per param with a tweakCount (repeated same-path edits fold into one row keeping first-before/last-after — a wiggled-14-times knob is the struggle signal), note/hit edits clustered per track and musical bar ("notes m1-2: 2 added"), clip automation summarized per (clip, param) with peak delta, structural facts as prose, tracks ordered by edit mass and params by tweaks-then-delta. Prose by default; `--json` emits the structured Rollup (or the raw DiffEntry[]). Vocabulary is the GUI/CLI's own (SynthPanel param keys, track ids) so the owner reads it untranslated right after a session. Pure rollupDiff/formatRollup in core; unit-tested (pin+ref resolution, repeat-collapse, --json shape) plus a CLI pilot. | ✅ done | ✅ done | — | ✅ Done | [`128-agent-owner-gui-loop.md`](research/128-agent-owner-gui-loop.md) | — |
| Edit telemetry — opt-in append-only edit-stream JSONL (log-don't-train) | research/116 §4 / research/128 §2.4, operationalized: an opt-in, append-only record of every document edit across all three write surfaces (GUI daemon, CLI, MCP), written to ~/.dotbeat/edit-log.jsonl OUTSIDE any project repo (the beat-scores.jsonl posture) so a working tree never carries telemetry. Off by default; `BEAT_EDIT_LOG=1` or `beat daemon --edit-log` turns it on for produce-song sessions. Schema per entry: {t, session (per-process id), surface (gui|cli|mcp), op, path, before, after, file}. Hook design (documented in edit-log.ts): NOT an observer inside the pure edit.ts (setValue fires dozens of times per debounced knob drag and gesture boundaries are a daemon concept — a pure-lib observer would log 60/sec and still miss the CLI/MCP verbs that call addNote/quantize directly), but ONE shared module hit at each surface's write moment, deriving entries from diffDocuments(before, after) — the project's own changeset vocabulary. GUI gestures ride the EXISTING undo-stack gesture boundary so a drag logs one entry (before=gesture start, after=drag settled), not one per tick. Surface attribution is honest: daemon edits are 'gui' only when browser-originated (Origin/Sec-Fetch headers) or unspecified, with a body `source` self-attribution escape hatch for a programmatic client. Unit-tested: schema, gesture coalescing to one entry, zero cost when disabled. | ✅ done | ✅ done | — | ✅ Done | [`116-daw-automation-sota.md`](research/116-daw-automation-sota.md) | — |

## Vary / audition loop

| Feature | Description | Core | CLI/MCP | GUI | Status | Research | Plan |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Rungs 1–3: vary / score / suggest | Generate parameter variants, audition live, keep or undo; a cold-start recommender picks the next group to try. | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ Done | — | [`phase-15-vary-affordance.md`](phase-15-vary-affordance.md) |
| Lane-aware drum vary groups (kick/snare/hats groups are an audio no-op on declared-lane tracks) | Pilot 101's high-severity finding, verified three ways (raw variant diffs, mutation-basis analysis, engine read): the kick/snare/hats vary groups mutate LEGACY track-wide drum-voice params (src/vary/vary.ts) that ui/src/audio/engine.ts provably never plays for a track with declared lanes — and declared 12-lane kits are what every fresh drums track gets since Phase 25, so the flagship "vary the kick" use case generates variants that all sound identical. Worse, the whole loop reinforces the illusion: score records the fake picks, suggest recommends more of the winning no-op group, and no inspect surface shows per-lane params that would reveal it. Fix needs lane-aware mutation groups (vary targets a lane's own voice params on declared-lane tracks, legacy params only on legacy tracks) plus a lane-param edit surface and inspect visibility — a real stream, not a patch. Fixed (Phase 35 Stream OA): on declared-lane tracks `beat vary`/beat_vary target a NAMED LANE's own backing params (synth voice tune/punch/decay/tone or sample start/length/AHD/filter/gainDb/tune — any declared lane, not just the historic five), emitted as replayable `<track>.lane.<name>.<param>` beat-set paths (a new core set-path, closing that gap too); legacy group names ERROR loudly there, naming the real lanes; legacy tracks bit-for-bit unchanged; suggest is lane-aware and skips history rated on inaudible differences. Verified end-to-end: adopt replay via beat set reproduces the variant byte-identically. | ✅ done | ✅ done | — | ✅ Done | [`101-usability-pilot-mcp-taste-loop.md`](research/101-usability-pilot-mcp-taste-loop.md) | [`phase-35-plan.md`](phase-35-plan.md) |

Each variant is a full document (humanize rewrites many note/hit fields, not a small edit list) generated by POST /vary-feel (read-only, selection-scoped, reuses varyTrack's enforced-scope guarantee) and previewed live via setDoc; Keep resends the variant's reproducible seed to POST /vary-feel/commit, which regenerates the identical content deterministically and writes it. And adopting a winner is a real verb now: `beat adopt <batch-dir> <pick>` / beat_adopt copies the picked variant over the parent through the manifest, refusing if the parent's sha256 no longer matches the batch's parentSha256 (the parent moved on) unless forced — pilot 101's "a feel winner is unadoptable MCP-only" gap closed with data-safety included. | ✅ done | ✅ done | — | ✅ Done | [`101-usability-pilot-mcp-taste-loop.md`](research/101-usability-pilot-mcp-taste-loop.md) | [`phase-35-plan.md`](phase-35-plan.md) |

| T0: instrumented score log + eval harness + blind audition | Score-log enrichment: `beat score` now measures EVERY rendered variant (picks and rejects) into the entry as a stable 13-dim DSP feature vector (src/taste/features.ts), making the log self-contained training data after batch dirs are deleted; `beat taste-eval --backfill` derives features for older entries whose renders survive. Eval harness (`beat taste-eval`, src/taste/eval.ts): leave-one-batch-out held-out pick prediction — top-1/top-3/pairwise vs analytic chance floors — with built-in reference scorers (random; dsp-bt = the v0 regularized logistic Bradley-Terry ranker over within-batch z-scored features, src/taste/ranker.ts, which also reports its learned taste directions). Blind audition: vary/gen `--audition` now shuffles presentation order (seeded from the batch seed, reproducible; `--no-shuffle` opts out) so listening position decouples from generation order, and a new `beat audition <dir>` stitches a scoreable clip-set batch from ANY directory of wavs (the T3 blind chop-rating flow) — score works on it, adopt correctly refuses. 11 tests (test/taste.test.ts) incl. a planted-taste recovery check; verified end-to-end on a live vary batch. | ✅ done | ✅ done | — | ✅ Done | [`107-taste-model-program.md`](research/107-taste-model-program.md) | [`taste-loop-design.md`](taste-loop-design.md) |

Proven by a planted-taste test where DSP features are held constant and only the embedding can see the preference — embed-bt orders >80% of held-out pairs while dsp-bt sits at chance. Per-variant-type ablation splits added 2026-07-17: every scorer's held-out folds are also attributed to their batch's round kind (vary / gen / clip-set, classified from the D21 manifest conventions), so the report shows where the model is actually predictive instead of one pooled number — splits partition the same folds (counts sum to the overall), small splits are labeled smoke (text tag + JSON smoke field). Piloted same-day (research/110: planted vary-taste read 75-100% top-1 while random gen picks sat at chance — the split answers "where does the model work" from one command); pilot fixes: --log now stands alone without a positional file, unknown flags error loudly, per-type sub-lines carry top-3. (2) HIGH — the "-1 dBTP ceiling" wording promised more than the deliberately upward-only cap delivers: capped lines now print wanted gain, applied gain, and measured true peak ("v1 +0.0 dB applied (wanted +14.9, capped: already at +0.4 dBTP)"), a batch whose renders exceed the ceiling on their own gets an explicit hot-render note, and help/MCP wording says "upward gains capped at -1 dBTP true peak — an already-hot render is never attenuated". (3) MEDIUM-HIGH — taste-collect's solo mix batches mutated `volume` while normalization cancelled it (±15 dB of silent gain; the rater never heard the difference the log recorded): solo mix batches now pin volume via the new vary `--exclude` (volume-level differences ARE the loudness confound the program controls away, docs/taste-loop-design.md "Confounds"), and an explicit `beat vary ... mix` with normalization on warns that volume differences will be normalized away. (4) MEDIUM — `suggest --taste-next` deduped (same gen command printed twice), no longer starves under-covered feel/drum-voice splits (concrete vary commands against the collection's seeds), and prepends `beat taste-seeds` when the dir has none (taste-collect errors without seeds even for --gen). Fourth sibling on the analyze/gen/embed sidecar template: stdlib-only top level, lazy surgepy import, --doctor / --list-patches / stdin-JSON render modes, exit codes 0/2/3/4; TS half (src/analysis/surge.ts) owns spawn+contract, showdown.ts owns the pure logic (role->category map, seeded pick, note-list conversion), the CLI orchestrates and degrades to warn-and-skip when surgepy is absent (never breaks the batch). LICENSING: Surge XT is GPLv3 but runs as an out-of-process render tool (outputs carry no code copyleft); the factory-patch CONTENT license is unresolved upstream (surge #6741), so surge-bearing batches get the same generated .gitignore as ref batches and the shared scores log stays kind-only. SETUP REALITY (confirmed 2026-07-21): surgepy has NO PyPI wheel — it is a CMake build target of Surge XT itself (-DSURGE_BUILD_PYTHON_BINDINGS=TRUE); `beat showdown --surge-doctor` reports availability + factory path + patch count and, when absent, the exact build steps (python/README.md). The full source build was not completed in the probe, so the smoke render is pending that build; the feature ships doctor-honest and works the moment surgepy is built. 11 tests (test/showdown.test.ts +6, test/surge-sidecar.test.ts new). | ✅ done | ✅ done | — | ✅ Done | [`114-synth-engine-alternatives.md`](research/114-synth-engine-alternatives.md) | [`source-showdown-eval.md`](source-showdown-eval.md) |
| Source-showdown: Surge factory-patch curation (surge-curated.json) | D26's first lever: `pickSurgePatch` used to draw BLIND from the whole ~639-preset factory pool, so a showdown surge clip was as likely to land on a thin or ugly patch as a good one. `scripts/curate-surge-patches.mjs` scores every role-appropriate patch on a short deterministic probe render (bass = sustained low note + its octave; chords = one held triad; lead = a 4-note motif) through `python/surge_render.py`, then GATES on two hard rejects (ringDb > -32 = a piercing narrow resonance; activeFraction < 0.5 = a mostly-silent render) and ranks survivors by a documented aesthetics-weighted z-score blend: 0.45 Audiobox quality (CE+PQ) + 0.30 ensemble-critic pessimistic score (criticWithUncertainty over the owner's rated dataset, READ-ONLY) + 0.15 ring headroom + 0.10 active fraction. The top quartile per role lands in `presets/surge-curated.json` ({name, category, relPath, scores} in deterministic order); `beat showdown --with-surge` loads it and draws the surge clip only from the curated pool (stderr-notes the per-role pool size), falling back cleanly to the full factory pool when the file is absent (CI-safe). Pure gate/blend/selection math + the curated-file<->pick glue live in the unit-tested `src/taste/surgeCuration.ts`; renders/scores cache under `~/Documents/dotbeat/tools/surge-curation-cache` (outside the repo) so re-runs are incremental. 11 tests (test/surge-curation.test.ts). | ✅ done | ✅ done | — | ✅ Done | — | [`decisions.md`](decisions.md) |

TS half src/taste/midifig.ts validates + converts to the ComposedPhrase contract: purely CHROMATIC transposition aimed by RELATIVE-KEY alignment (same mode -> seed tonic; cross-mode -> the seed's relative minor/major, so diatonic notes land in the seed scale with the commercial intervals untouched), whole-octave register recentring, per-source distinct files exclude-chained (same contract as the bank; --shared-figure shares one), batch bpm from the midi's own tempo when no ref pins it, warn-and-fall-back-to-bank on anything unusable. LICENSING (the ref-chop posture, enforced): .mid transcriptions are derivative works — they live outside any repo, midi-figure batches get the generated .gitignore gate, the manifest records path+transposition as local reference only, and the shared scores log records ONLY figureSource:'midi'|'bank' (new manifest/ScoreEntry field via scoreBatch), never a song identity. 19 tests (test/midifig.test.ts) incl. sidecar runs against an ORIGINAL in-repo fixture (test/fixtures/midifig-house.mid, composed in code); stub CLI pilot verified per-source distinct commercial figures, gitignore gates, and a leak-free log. LOCAL CMA-ES from a curated/factory preset toward the per-role SCALAR targets research/131 measured — a categorically easier problem than T6's full-spectrum matching, and structurally unlike the T5 scaling gate because there is no large space to search: every parameter starts at the preset's own value and may move at most one declared TRUST RADIUS. src/retarget/ holds the four pieces: features.ts (the discriminator axes the frozen 13-key FEATURE_KEYS cannot see — flux, per-band crest, flatness by region, spectral tilt, onset/attack statistics, S/M width), targets.ts (per-role profiles whose thresholds are either a number 131 §7 / 138 §2 states in words or a quantile of the owner's own pack-ref pool, with weights = 131 §3.1's measured paired-d; widthMeanDb reported but NEVER scored since engine renders are mono), loss.ts (a worst-axis power mean over misses clipped at zero once satisfied — the anti-gaming half — plus a quadratic surcharge for breaking a target the preset already had, an identity-drift term, and a trust-region term), and harness.ts (CMA-ES in a trust cube where each dimension maps onto its own radius, preset evaluated first as the anchor so a retarget can never report worse than its input). Runners for both backends; both write a bank in engine-curated.json's shape with per-row provenance and a held-out-figure check. Measured: engine 1.02 s/render (8 s clip), surge 0.52 s serial and 0.20 s at 8-way parallel — surge is the CHEAPER backend, opposite the going assumption. Also fixed en route: surge_render.py's `overrides` path validated "normalized 0..1" while surgepy's setParamVal takes NATIVE values, so it could only reach 440.00-466.16 Hz of a 13.75 Hz-14 kHz cutoff range; `nativeOverrides` + `--dump-params` added alongside (the old path untouched — cached renders are keyed on it). 39 tests. | Produced defaults: generated projects ship with role-aware production, not a dry/mono init patch (plan A1) | The blind source-showdown found the engine's synth loses to commercial chops on production RICHNESS, not cleanliness (mono output ≈ -52 dB stereo width vs ≈ -11 dB for real records, near-zero air-band energy, lowest production-COMPLEXITY scores while production-QUALITY was flat). An ablation adding ONLY production edits — same notes, same picked samples — moved the engine from 3% to 29% of blind pairwise wins (63% on lead). The format already owned all the DSP; nothing in the generation path had ever touched it. New shared module src/analysis/produce.ts (productionRoleFor -> productionProfileFor -> applyProducedDefaults, grounded in research/115 P1 width / P2 air / P3 motion) applies a role-appropriate width/air/glue/space/motion layer to every registered track: the drum bus (kit) gets air shelf + light glue only (it carries the kick — §2.2 withholds width/reverb/auto-pan at the bus); bass gets saturation + a sidechain duck under the kit's kick (§4.2) but NO width and NO reverb send (mono-anchored low end); lead gets the full chorus/saturation/reverb+delay-sends/air-shelf stack; hats/perc get air + reverb + auto-pan when they stand alone. Two invariants keep it safe on-by-default: intensify-only (Math.max against the patch's own value — never quiets an already-produced patch, re-applying is a no-op, so the taste loop searches FROM a produced start) and deterministic (pure function of role, no rng — preserves gen-kit's byte-identical-per-seed recipe property). Wired into gen-kit (cli/beat.mjs genKitCmd) and the taste seeds (src/taste/seeds.ts, at a scaled-down seed tier with seed-derived jitter so vary keeps headroom both ways); the showdown's engineplus ablation clip delegates to the same primitive (frozen constants). Stub gen-kit before/after (seed 7): stereo width -19.1 -> -16.9 dB, centroid 560 -> 713 Hz, -18.9 -> -13.8 LUFS. Track 1b makes the production layer a first-class AUTHORING move: `beat add-track <file> <id> <kind> --produced [--role bass|lead|pad|keys|drums]` creates the track then applies its role's produced-defaults profile the moment it exists, and `beat produce <file> <track> [--role r] [--dry-run]` retrofits an EXISTING track ("make this track engineplus" as one command). The role is inferred from the track id via the same productionRoleFor synonym table gen-kit uses (an un-inferrable drums id falls back to the kit bus, not the mild default); --role overrides, its user aliases mapping through the same table (keys->chords, drums->kit) with the resolved role echoed in the receipt. Both flow through the SAME src/analysis/produce.ts primitives (new shared resolveProducedProfile/kickSourceTrack helpers) — zero duplicated production VALUES — and inherit the layer's two safety invariants: intensify-only (Math.max against the patch's own value — never weakens an already-produced patch, re-running is a no-op) and deterministic (pure function of role). A produced bass/sub is augmented with the §4.2 sidechain duck under a kick-carrying drums track when one exists (the one move a base profile can't set itself); non-voiced instrument/audio kinds are refused with a clear message; synth-only moves (osc-bank width, utility/auto-pan inserts) correctly withhold on drum/sample voices and the honest applied-list receipt reflects exactly that. It has now found three defects no whole-file gate could see: the bass growl arriving 14 dB under the mix at a nominal -5 dB fader and the click 49 dB under at -16 (2026-07-26); and the chords pad arriving 10.93 dB under the mix at the SECOND HIGHEST fader in its stack, which is the owner's "The dark pad on the layered-after is not loud enough and is barely audible" (chords 1147, same day). In every case `verifyLayeredTargets` was flat or preferred the defective clip — on the chords pair it scores the BEFORE at 3/7 and the fixed AFTER at 2/7, because articulationDb correctly reads a newly-audible sustained bed as filling the gaps between notes. Banked as listen-bench/cases/2026-07-26-layered-chords-buried-pad-thin-stab.json, whose `metricGap` field is this row. WHY IT IS NOT ALREADY A GATE, stated so the cost is visible: this is a MULTI-TRACK feature by construction — it needs N+1 renders per clip instead of 1 — so it cannot join MixMetrics, which reads one finished WAV. The decision to be taken is whether the layered arm's verification renders per layer. TRIGGER: none needed. | A gated stab as the ONLY voice over the body: `body+stab` band share | FOUND AND DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED, 2026-07-26, so it does not become a tail nobody comes back for. A chords `body+stab` draw is one SUSTAINED low layer plus one GATED mid layer (maxDurationSteps 2-4, sustain 0.05), so whole-file band share reads the body almost alone whatever the stab's fader says: seed 7201 measures 56.1% bass-band against the refs-packs chords p75 of 47.2% and 41.3% mids against a p25 of 50.4%, AFTER its level floor was raised 2 dB (it was 61.4% before). Raising the fader further is not the fix — it was measured and it moved the number 5 points without clearing the band. The real question is a design one this stream declined to guess at: should a gated accent ever be the only voice above the body, or does `body+stab` need either a sustained tail on the stab or a non-optional pad? Both answers are defensible and both change the family weights in CHORD_FAMILIES. | The drums bus lowpass is now CHOSEN: fresh tracks opened, the rated 8 kHz regime named and frozen | FOUND 2026-07-27 producing songs/twin-souls-study; FIXED the same day, but NOT the way the finding first proposed — read the judgment call before opening anything. On a drums-kind track `synth.cutoff` is not a synth filter, it is the WHOLE KIT'S BUS LOWPASS at 24 dB/oct, and two separate literals sat in that slot with nobody having chosen either. REPRODUCED INDEPENDENTLY (seed-001 copied to scratch, `beat render --stems` then `beat metrics --json`, only that value changed): 8000 → air 0.77% / centroid 194 Hz; 20000 → air 1.92% / centroid 359 Hz. Directionally identical to the original 0.72/177 vs 2.15/389 and inside the metrics' own declared run-to-run variance, so the finding stands: roughly two-thirds of the air band and half the centroid, against a Demucs commercial drum stem at 7.40% / 862 Hz, on drum-loop — the one role the engine has never placed top-half in (0 of 27 batches). WHAT SHIPPED. (1) Fresh drums tracks: 12000 → 18000, exported as `DRUMS_TRACK_INIT_CUTOFF_HZ`. The old 12000 mirrored `new Tone.Filter({ frequency: 12000 })` in ui/src/audio/engine.ts:948 — a CONSTRUCTION placeholder the engine overwrites from the document one line later — so it was never a musical decision; 18000 is the format's own documented wide-open value, and the change has zero eval exposure because no blind rating has ever been given to a `beat add-track` render. (2) The taste-seed 8000 is NOT opened. It is named `TASTE_DRUMS_BUS_CUTOFF_HZ` and FROZEN, with `TASTE_DRUMS_BUS_CUTOFF_OPEN_HZ` (18000) added ALONGSIDE as an opt-in named regime plus `beat taste-seeds --drums-bus frozen|open`, which prints the regime and warns that `open` is not batch-for-batch comparable. THE REASON IT IS FROZEN is a fact the original finding did not have: the lowpass is applied ASYMMETRICALLY across the arms being compared — the engine arm (seed drums soloed) and the GEN arm (same drums track, one-shots swapped into its lanes; drum-loop is keymap kind `kit`) both render through it, while the REF arm is a refs-packs wav copied straight in and never touches it, and presets/engine-curated.json has no drums entry so the engine arm can never override it. That makes 8000 a treatment entangled in all 27 rated batches, i.e. CLAUDE.md's frozen-eval rule in its exact spirit: editing the literal would hand future batches a different treatment while `beat showdown --report` keeps pooling them with the old 27 under one `showdown:drum-loop` key. Guarded by the D-LP block in test/eval-integrity.test.ts (4 tests), whose load-bearing assertion is structural rather than a pinned answer — the scored `air` band is 6 kHz..Nyquist and OPEN ABOVE, so a rated drums bus may not lowpass into it unnamed; it fails on the real pre-fix 12000 and 3 of 4 fire on a simulated silent re-key of the rated regime. STILL OPEN: opening the filter reaches 1.92% against a commercial 7.40%, so the cap was never the whole drum deficit, and the PREFERENCE effect remains unmeasured. TRIGGER: the next drum-loop showdown round — `beat taste-seeds <dir> --drums-bus open`, everything else pinned (verified frozen and open seeds differ in exactly one line of emitted text), pairing batch-for-batch against the existing 27. | ✅ done | ✅ done | — | ✅ Done | — | — |
| `keymapScratchText` hosts the gen arm at 8 kHz while its identical sibling documents 18 kHz as correct | FOUND 2026-07-27 while naming the drums-bus regimes, and deliberately NOT fixed in that pass so it does not become an unmeasured edit to a rated arm. `keymapScratchText` (src/taste/showdown.ts) is the scratch host the GEN arm uses for the pitched roles — bassline, chords, lead — and it renders the generated one-shot through a drums-kind bus lowpassed at 8000 Hz. Twelve hundred lines below it in the SAME FILE, `surgeSampleHostText` does the identical job (a neutral drums-kind host whose only purpose is to play a generated sample back transparently) at `cutoff 18000`, with the comment "deliberately NEUTRAL — a wide-open filter ... so the only colour the production pass adds is its own". By that file's own stated design intent the keymap host should be 18000 too, and its 8000 is a copy-paste that predates the surge host. WHY IT WAS LEFT: unlike the drums-kit case there is NO measurement of what it costs — the 0.77%→1.92% air numbers were taken on a kit, not on a pitched one-shot — and it colours the gen arm of already-rated pitched batches, so changing it silently would repeat the original defect in the opposite direction. It is now at least visible: the value references the shared `TASTE_DRUMS_BUS_CUTOFF_HZ` constant, so the two rated hosts are provably one number and the D-LP guard covers it. ## Owner-agent loop

| Feature | Description | Core | CLI/MCP | GUI | Status | Research | Plan |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Option boards: `beat board` non-blind production picking surface (research/128) | The NON-BLIND sibling of `beat rate` (research/128 §2.1, build item 1): where `rate` is blind MEASUREMENT into beat-scores.jsonl, `beat board <dir>` is named PRODUCTION picking into a DELIBERATELY SEPARATE beat-decisions.jsonl (D24: production never contaminates eval; the two logs share a directory, never a file). A self-contained node:http + inline-HTML page (same no-build, no-React pattern as rate.mjs) over every UNDECIDED rendered batch under <dir>: each board shows its 2-4 candidates in manifest order (small-N warning above 4) with VISIBLE provenance in the GUI/manifest vocabulary verbatim (source.kind / recipe / provider / from / edits / media) + the measured features table (LUFS/crest/centroid/width/band shares from computeBatchFeatures), solo audio players (a solo/in-context toggle when every candidate has a vN.context.wav), and three verbs — PICK one (1-9, optional note), REJECT ALL (n, note REQUIRED: the reject-with-feedback channel, doc 128 §2.6), or SKIP (s, records nothing durable). Styling borrows the daemon GUI's design tokens (ui/src/styles.css: amber accent, dark panels, mono readouts, light/dark) so moving between the GUI and a board doesn't feel like leaving the product — the standalone sibling of the in-GUI VaryAffordance bar. A pick/reject writes <batch>/decision.json (the per-batch answer an agent polls on resume) + one append to beat-decisions.jsonl carrying a provenance snapshot + feature vector (the trainable, non-blind accept/reject labels 116/128 said were evaporating). Agent-side: `beat board <dir> --status [--json]` (no server) prints per-batch decided/undecided + decisions — how the produce-song loop checks for answers. Decision core is src/board/decisions.ts, kept separate from the page so a future daemon route / in-GUI panel can back the same machinery. Adopt a winner with the unchanged `beat adopt <batch> <pick>`. 8 tests (test/board.test.ts: append-only log, decided-batch scan, reject-all-needs-a-note, decision.json shape, the beat-scores separation invariant, provenance+features snapshot, --status text + --json). | ✅ done | ✅ done | — | ✅ Done | [`128-agent-owner-gui-loop.md`](research/128-agent-owner-gui-loop.md) | — |

## Render / export

| Feature | Description | Core | CLI/MCP | GUI | Status | Research | Plan |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GUI Export button | Reuses the live engine’s own capture path; verified against the CLI reference render. | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ done | ✅ Done | — | [`phase-20-render-export.md`](phase-20-render-export.md) |
| Per-track stem rendering (`beat render --stems`) | DONE (Phase 37 Stream RA): `beat render <file> --stems [--out-dir d]` writes one solo WAV per track, reusing the existing `renderTrackSolosCommand` store-solo session (one browser boot for all stems). Feeds the D2 metrics/lint loop with per-stem signal, not just mix-bus. | ✅ done | ✅ done | — | ✅ Done | [`52-ableton-vs-dotbeat-files-and-sets.md`](research/52-ableton-vs-dotbeat-files-and-sets.md) | [`phase-37-plan.md`](phase-37-plan.md) |
| `beat feedback --offline` (the arrangement gate can run without a live browser) | DONE 2026-07-27. FOUND producing songs/twin-souls-study: `beat render` grew `--offline` (D22/D23) and it is exact and reproducible, but `beat feedback` re-rendered through REAL-TIME CAPTURE only, so verifying a full-length song cost its wall-clock duration with a live headless browser held open the whole time — on a 5:22 song, 5:22 of fragility, and two of three attempts died with `Target page, context or browser has been closed` when the machine slept. It matters more than an ordinary speed complaint because `feedback --sections --ref` is THE gate that checks arrangement against a measured reference arc, the one thing no static whole-file lint can see. SHIPPED: `beat feedback <file> [--offline | --live]` plus `beat_feedback`'s `offline` option, spelled and validated exactly as `beat render`'s are. The row predicted "plumbing, not design: renderToBuffer already takes the offline path" — it did NOT; `renderToBuffer` hard-coded `captureWav` and now takes `{ offline }`. The offline/live DECISION (flags mutually exclusive; an asked-for --offline on a refusing project is a hard error while a merely-defaulted one falls back with the reason kept; the chosen mode is announced) was a second copy across `beat render` and `beat render --batch` and is now one shared policy, src/render/capture-mode.ts, that all three surfaces import — a third copy would have been the seventh measured instance of the drift CLAUDE.md's parity rule exists to stop. `feedback` also left cli-surface.test.ts's UNKNOWN_FLAG_HOLES ledger, so a typo'd `--offlin` is loud instead of silently running the non-exact path (pilot 109's bug on a new flag). VERIFIED end to end on the 5:22 song, both paths, same reference arc: live 345.1s wall clock, offline 250.3s (229.9s compute for 322.6s of audio = 1.4x realtime); both `arc check: PASS`, 9/9 sections within 2.3 LU, per-section arc deltas agreeing to within 0.2 LU. The static per-section numbers do NOT agree as closely, and the gap EXCEEDS the documented render-run variance floor: width -8.1 dB on the intro and -4.6 dB on the outro (floor 1.5 dB), crest -1.2 dB on s_drop3 (floor 1.0 dB), centroid -6 to -40 Hz with no floor defined at all — and directionally, offline is darker on 9/9 sections and lower-crest on 7/9, never higher. That is a systematic bias, not noise; it has its own roadmap row ("Offline vs live is a systematic spectral bias") and it is the reason NOT to compare a whole-mix `--ref` profile across paths. DEFAULT-FOR-LONG-SONGS: proposed by this row, REJECTED on measurement. Offline was faster here, but length does not predict the ratio (1.4x on this song vs 0.31x on examples/first-light after D23 — offline compute scales with length x density, not length), and a length-triggered default would mean two songs either side of 2 minutes are measured through different chains and one song crossing it silently changes chains mid-project — unacceptable for a gate that diffs against a saved profile. Instead a song over 2 minutes prints a note naming `--offline` and renders live anyway; whichever path you pick, you keep. Guards (test/capture-mode.test.ts, 13 tests) verified by mutation, not assumption. The CLI pilot run on the finished flag (2026-07-27) independently reproduced the cross-path width gap on its own tiny project (live -44.0/-55.6 dB vs offline -58.8/-60.9 dB per section) while its offline-saved arc profile still PASSed against a LIVE render with deltas <=0.2 LU — the same split, twice, on two different projects. It also produced two fixes taken in this stream: `--sections` on a loop-mode project used to render the WHOLE song and only then error (10.5s wall for a 7.6s loop; now refused from the parse in ~0.2s, with the timing itself asserted), and the offline refusal now names the way out ("drop --offline (or pass --live)") on all three surfaces at once, since they share one policy. Its remaining findings are separate backlog rows. | ✅ done | ✅ done | — | ✅ Done | — | — |
| Fast render, slice 1: one harness boot per vary batch (`render --batch`) | DONE: `beat render --batch <dir>` boots daemon+vite+Chromium ONCE and renders every variant by swapping the watched file and waiting for the store to hot-reload — measured 33s vs ~70s for a 12-variant batch (boot was most of every per-variant render). `beat vary --render` and `beat source gen` ride it automatically. Since D23, batch renders default to OFFLINE compute (short clips are exactly where it is both exact and fast — verified ~1.0x realtime per real-groove variant even on the weak dev container), decided once per batch and printed loudly: soundfont projects fall back to live capture with the refusal reason printed, --live forces realtime capture, --offline errors instead of falling back. | ✅ done | ✅ done | — | ✅ Done | — | — |
| Fast render, slice 2: offline compute (`beat render --offline`, D22) | DONE (opt-in): the SAME Engine class constructed inside a Tone OfflineContext — no MediaRecorder/opus loss, metrics repeatable run-to-run and oscillator content to ~1 LSB (noise-based voices vary per run offline AND live — Tone.Noise is unseeded; pilot 109 quantified both), parity-gated against live capture on smoke + real-groove within src/metrics/variance.ts bounds (the two exceptions — mono-project "width", sub-band tilt — are the live capture chain's own lossiness). Refuses soundfont projects loudly at parse time; bitcrushRate degrades to passthrough with a named caveat. NOT unconditionally fast: Tone schedules the whole song then renders once, so spent one-shot voices accumulate and compute grows superlinearly with length×density (smoke 3.4x realtime; 8-track 96s first-light 0.12-0.32x — CLI prints the ratio + a heads-up under 1x). See D22 for the three t=0/context invariants this surfaced. Pilot 109 same-day CLI fixes: refusal used to hang the process forever (exitCode-without-exit + leaked handles), every render leaked its vite preview server (~90MB each), unknown flags were silently swallowed (a typo'd --offlin silently ran the NON-exact live path), --offline was silently dropped with --stems, and --preview-port was ignored in stems mode. | ✅ done | ✅ done | — | ✅ Done | [`109-usability-pilot-render-offline.md`](research/109-usability-pilot-render-offline.md) | — |
| Offline render scaling: schedule-window + dispose-behind-frontier (D23) | DONE: the offline context is now a raw NATIVE OfflineAudioContext (same raw-native pinning the live engine does for spessasynth — standardized-audio-context snapshots the graph at startRendering, which both silenced post-window-1 scheduling and made scheduling itself crawl), rendered in ~2s windows via native suspend()/resume(): Tone's clock runs one window ahead of the render frontier, and spent one-shot sources are disposed once the frontier has actually rendered past their stop time — per-quantum cost O(sounding notes) instead of O(every note so far). Measured: real-groove 27s -> 9.0s; first-light 10s/30s was 0.32x->0.12x (quadratic), now 0.31x flat (linear). Parity gates unchanged (smoke/real-groove/voxtest). Runtime-checked internals hooks with a loud one-pass fallback; decimator forced passthrough on all offline contexts (native ones expose createScriptProcessor but never pause for its events). | ✅ done | ✅ done | — | ✅ Done | — | — |
| Offline vs live is a systematic spectral bias, not run-to-run noise — and it is over the variance floor | FOUND 2026-07-27 by three independent measurements on the same day, while shipping `beat feedback --offline`. D22 lists "mono-project width" and "sub-band tilt" as the two known offline-vs-live parity exceptions; the measurements say that framing understates it. Rendering songs/twin-souls-study (5:22, 9 sections) through BOTH paths and differencing per section: LUFS agreed to 0.10 LU (floor 0.25) and band shares to 2 pt (floor 2.0) — but stereo width was off by 8.1 dB on the intro and 4.6 dB on the outro against a 1.5 dB floor (5.4x over), crest by 1.2 dB on s_drop3 against a 1.0 dB floor, and spectral centroid by 6-40 Hz (-0.7% to -4.3%) with NO tolerance defined for centroid anywhere in src/metrics/variance.ts. The DIRECTION is what makes it a bias rather than scatter: centroid lower offline on 9 of 9 sections, crest lower on 7 of 9, never higher on either. Offline renders DARKER and lower-crest. Confirmed twice more the same day: an independent full-mix check (centroid 547 offline vs 573 live, air 2.21% vs 2.40%, crest 12.8 vs 14.2 — that crest gap also over the floor) and a drums-BUS render where the gap is far larger (air 0.32% offline vs 0.77% live, centroid 116 Hz vs 194 Hz at the same cutoff), i.e. the magnitude is MATERIAL-dependent: small on a dense full mix, large on isolated drums. That is the dangerous shape — the error is not bounded by anything the caller can see, so a stem screened offline can read as "too dark" purely from the capture path. WHAT IS NOT AFFECTED: the arc gate. `feedback --sections --ref` compares RELATIVE LUFS and the two paths agreed to within 0.2 LU per section, inside the floor; `--offline` is trustworthy there and that is why it shipped. WHAT OWES: (1) a cross-path characterization the way Phase 34 Stream NC characterized run-to-run variance (scripts/measure-render-determinism.mjs is the template) — per-metric offline-vs-live bias across material types, not one song; (2) a centroid entry in variance.ts, which has none; (3) a decision on whether the bias is offline being WRONG or live being lossy (D22 assumes the latter — the opus/MediaRecorder chain — but nothing has isolated it, and the drums-bus magnitude is large enough to matter musically); (4) a loud refusal or warning when a whole-mix `--ref` profile is compared against a render from the other capture path, which today is silent. TRIGGER: none needed — the next stem-level or reference-profile comparison across paths hits this. | 🔶 partial | 🔶 partial | — | ⬜ Not started | — | — |
