## Sampling toolkit

| Feature | Description | Core | CLI/MCP | GUI | Status | Research | Plan |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `beat chop` — beat/bar/section-aware cutting into a clip-set batch | Research 142 §2.1, "the highest-value new verb in this document." The finding loop before this: open the file in something that isn't dotbeat, scrub, guess at bar lines, export a region by hand, `beat sample` it (with no provenance), discover it's off-grid, repeat — four of five steps labor. Everything upstream (`beat analyze`'s cached *.analysis.json) and downstream (`beat board`/`beat audition`/`beat rate`, which read any dir with a clip-set manifest) already existed; the cut was the only missing piece. `beat chop <wav> [--grid bar|beat|section] [--bars N] [--max N] [--out <dir>] [--analysis <file>] [--backend ...] [--license "..."]` writes chop-<name>/ with c001.wav …, one <chop>.wav.json provenance sidecar each (the enforced media/<id>.wav.json shape + derivedFrom{file,sha256,start,end}, bpm, grid, downbeat index, section label), a chops.json index, a clip-set manifest, and a generated .gitignore. Four design calls from the mined corpus, all asserted in test/chop.test.ts because each is easy to reverse: GRID-DEFAULT with no transient mode at all (over-segmentation is the corpus's named failure mode — "fewer chops... lets it breathe"); zero-crossing snap (±2 ms) + 3 ms seam fade, cut from the mono sum so channels cut at the same sample; NO normalize and NO silence-trim (the tests assert an ~8x level ratio between a loud and a quiet bar survives, and that a bar opening with a rest keeps the rest — normalizing destroys the between-chop relationship the owner is listening for, trimming pulls the chop early against the grid); and registers NOTHING (batch-then-adopt). planChops/cutChops are pure; the CLI owns I/O. No MCP twin yet. | ✅ done | ✅ done | undefined | 🚧 In progress | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| `beat resample` — bounce a track back in as a registered sample | Research 142 §3.1, the mined corpus's #1 unprompted pick: "the resample step is what converts 'a recording' into 'an instrument'." Two consequences the corpus states outright — bounce LONG then trim short ("you can always shorten notes... but you can't lengthen them any further than the duration of the original sample"), and run a degradation chain BEFORE the bounce specifically so it "can't be un-done or re-balanced later." `beat resample <file.beat> <track> [--out <sample-id>] [--license "..."]` is glue between renderTrackSolosCommand (already renders one solo WAV per track through one harness boot) and registerPreppedMedia (the enforced-provenance registration `beat source add`/`beat adopt` share). src/vary/resample.ts owns everything around the render so it is pure and testable with no harness. The sidecar records what matters once a chain is committed: the track, its kind, the document's sha256 at bounce time, and the ordered insert chain verbatim including bypassed members. Default id is a FRESH <track>-resample/-2/-3 (a second bounce is a new take, never a silent overwrite); an explicit --out may re-register and says so. Cost is printed BEFORE it is paid and stated in --help: ~10-15 s harness boot + roughly realtime capture of the WHOLE project (there is no range render — `beat excerpt` is the documented workaround). A deliberate act, not an inner loop. No MCP twin yet. | ✅ done | ✅ done | undefined | 🚧 In progress | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| Per-lane polyphony (`voices`) — keymap lanes stop choking themselves | Research 142 D6, found that pass and previously unattributed — a sixth keymap defect research 132 missed, and it was in Tone, not in keymap.ts. Every sample-backed lane was ONE Tone.Player, and Source.start() on an already-started source calls restart(), which stops the most recently created source: a repeated hit on one lane CUT ITS OWN TAIL. No legato, no overlap. Fine (and wanted) on a kick; wrong on a keymap, which is a pitched instrument built out of drum lanes, on exactly the decaying bells/plucks/pads that path generates — plausibly part of the 38% pairwise nobody had attributed. Fix: `voices`, an ordinary sample-lane param, DEFAULT 1 (today's behavior exactly, so every committed kit, golden render and showdown arm is bit-for-bit unchanged and self-choking stays the default where it belongs), with `beat keymap` minting its lanes at 4 and re-backing upgrading old ones. Engine carries a round-robin pool of player/filter/envGain/velGain per lane so the signal ORDER is unchanged from the single-voice path and each hit gets its own AHD envelope; one decoded buffer per lane still. MEASURED in ui/verify-lane-polyphony.mjs (5/5, real engine, a 1.5 s decaying bell hit twice 500 ms apart with a quiet second hit): with voices 1, adding the quiet second hit made the lane 6.85 dB QUIETER than not hitting it at all — the bug, in numbers; with voices 4 the tail survives and the pool gives 9.94 dB more energy at identical hits. That measurement also caught a second real bug: per-hit velocity was being written to the LANE-SHARED gain, so a quiet second hit attenuated the first hit's ringing tail even with a pool. Scope is D6 only — multi-zone / round-robin / velocity layers are keymap-v2 (142 §4.1) and are separate rows. | ✅ done | ✅ done | ❌ missing | 🚧 In progress | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| `beat stem-split` — expose the built-but-unreachable Demucs separator | Research 142 §1.4/§3.8/build item 1, sized XS and one of "two things to build first that are not on anyone's list." src/analysis/stems.ts + python/stem_extract.py are fully built and tested — four stems (bass/other/drums/vocals), a near-silence guard that reports what it fell back to and why, RMS receipts for kept/residual, htdemucs already in the shared venv — and reachable ONLY as a flag inside `beat source gen`. It is THE sampling move for found music: chop the drums out of a record, lift the vocal, isolate the bass before you keymap it. Needs a CLI surface, a --doctor, and a row in python/README.md's table. Nothing else. NOT built in the 142 stream (which took items 2/3/5 and D6); deferred with a trigger: build it the first time a real record is chopped and the wanted layer is buried in a full mix. | ✅ done | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| `beat slice` — one bar to N drum lanes via start/length (MPC slice mode) | Research 142 §3.3, size S, "the single move that turns a found break into PLAYABLE material rather than a loop you place." Needs no new files, no format change and no engine work: params.start/params.length already trim into the buffer per lane and the AHD envelope already shapes each slice, so N lanes backed by the SAME media id at different start/length IS a slice kit. What is missing is only the verb that computes the pairs — and gen-trim.ts's onset-flux detector already computes them. Ships alongside a real choke-group declaration (the hard-coded hat->openhat rule is not enough for a slice kit). Composes with `beat resample` (slice a resampled stack) and `beat chop` (slice one chop). Deferred with a trigger: build it once the owner has a chop set they want to REARRANGE rather than place. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| `beat audio-reverse` as a file op | Research 142 §3.4, size XS, ~10 lines over decodeWav/encodeWav16. Reverse is ubiquitous in the corpus (reverse-reverb: reverse the dry, reverb it, reverse back so the build-up precedes the transient; reversed crashes and risers as transitions; a credited compositional use on Kendrick Lamar's "Father Time"), research 121 §3.5 named "a reverse crash" specifically, and dotbeat cannot do it AT ALL — `beat reverse` is MIDI-only and nothing in src/, ui/src/ or cli/ reverses audio. 142 is explicit that the FILE OP is strictly better than a `reverse` field on BeatAudioRegion: AudioBufferSourceNode has no negative playbackRate, so the field means a format bump AND holding a reversed copy of every buffer, while the file op needs neither and COMPOSES — the reversed file is ordinary media you can then chop, keymap, slice or resample, and reverse-reverb becomes reverse -> place -> resample-with-reverb -> reverse in verbs that now all exist. Deferred with a trigger: build it the first time a transition needs a riser. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| Reach a drum lane's `filter=` / `fx=` from CLI and MCP | Research 142 §1.3/D11, size XS. A sample-backed lane's filter TYPE and its ordered playback-effect chain are expressible in the format and render correctly, but have NO CLI and NO MCP verb: setLaneParamPath rejects any key that isn't a SAMPLE_LANE_PARAM_KEY, addEffect targets tracks, and setLaneBacking (which would accept fx=) is wired only into the daemon's lane-ops route. So the drum-sampler's whole processing surface — and therefore every keymap's — is reachable only by hand-editing the file or through the GUI, and a keymap ships with a wide-open filter and no effects unless someone does. It is what makes a keymap PRODUCIBLE. Gets a CLI<->MCP parity-table row when built (the repo's own structural rule). | ✅ done | ❌ missing | ✅ done | ⬜ Not started | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| Chop ranking + provenance chips in `beat board` | Research 142 §2.2/§2.3, size S. A six-minute track at 128 bpm is ~180 bars, and nobody auditions 180 candidates — so the RANKING is the labor-removal, more than the cut is. computeBatchFeatures already measures every wav in a batch dir into the same feature vector beat board already displays, so `--rank distinct` (a greedy max-min-distance selection presenting the N most DIFFERENT chops first, collapsing near-duplicates into one representative with a "+7 similar" note) plus honest single-axis sorts (--rank loud|bright|tonal — tonal is the one that finds the chord stab in a drum-heavy record) and --rank order are mostly wiring. Board-side: render the chop provenance (source file, bar index, section label, start timecode) in the existing provenance-chip row, and page the top 9 rather than warning above 4. RANK, NEVER REJECT, and never with the taste model or a CLAP index — 142 §2.2 and D30 both forbid it (CLAP scored BELOW chance on intra-batch preference; the taste model has never seen a chop; ordering is safe because a mistake costs a scroll, rejection is not because a mistake costs the sample). Deferred with a trigger: build it the first time a chop set is large enough that scrolling it is the bottleneck — i.e. a real full-length record rather than a test loop. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| Raw ingest path (`--raw`) + chop-shaped provenance on `beat sample` | Research 142 §2.4, size S — the licensing hole, and it is one flag wide. media/<id>.wav.json is ENFORCED by registerPreppedMedia (it rolls the wav copy back if the sidecar write fails), but only on the beat source add / source gen / adopt / library-install paths: `beat sample` writes NO sidecar at all, so the single FASTEST way to get audio into a dotbeat project is also the only way that records nothing about where it came from. Two changes: a --raw ingest that skips prepOneshot's trim/fade/normalize (wrong for a bar of music — see the `beat chop` row) while KEEPING the sidecar, and bringing `beat sample` under the sidecar rule rather than leaving it an exception. `beat chop` already writes the chop-shaped fields (derivedFrom/bpm/grid/sectionLabel) so a chop's licence status inherits and stays auditable, which is the whole point when the source is a commercial record. D30 standing constraint 2. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| Blind arm: does PRODUCING a sampled chop move the owner's ear? | Research 142 build item 5's validation, and 142 §8's own words: "the load-bearing bet of this whole plan," entirely unmeasured. The audio-track production chain now exists and is measured to WORK (ui/verify-audio-track-fx.mjs), but whether a produced chop BEATS an unproduced one — and whether either enters the ref band — is a different question and a blind one. Pre-registered shape: chops from the refs-packs pool, produced vs unproduced, through the existing beat showdown / beat rate flow. D30's own revisit trigger is this arm coming back at or below the unproduced control, which would mean the manipulation surface, not the finding loop, was the wrong half to fund first. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| Sampled layer in layered.ts (`LayerSpec.source` as a union) | Research 142 §5, size M. buildLayeredClip assembles three or four SYNTH tracks from one shared ComposedPhrase, and LayerSpec.patch is typed Partial<BeatSynth>. A sampled layer needs that to become a discriminated union — and 142's finding is that the real blocker was never the union, it was the CROSSOVER: LayerBand is implemented as the track's one filter ("that single rule is what makes the sum a mix instead of four voices fighting"). A sample-backed drum lane HAS a filter, so a pitched or percussive sampled layer was already reachable through a drum-lane host; a loop/texture layer was blocked on the audio track having none — which the production-chain row above has now fixed. Remaining structure to respect: one drums TRACK per sampled layer, not one lane (per-lane effect TYPES are per-lane but their param VALUES come from the track's synth block, and monoViolations inspects track-level BeatSynth fields), and onset alignment is NOT free for a sample layer (a one-shot with 12 ms of leading silence puts its layer 12 ms late — measure the first sample above threshold and write it into the lane's params.start). What it unblocks is precisely what research 131 measured missing: packs put 60.1% of their energy below 60 Hz and engineplus bass puts 0.22% there — "the sub layer is a real recorded 808" is a sampled-layer move, and so is "the air layer is a reversed cymbal." Note src/taste/layered.ts has other active writers; coordinate before starting. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| keymap-v2a — multi-zone + root verification + pre-audition | Research 142 §4.1, size M, fixing D1/D2/D9/D11. Multi-zone needs NO format change — every lane already names its own sample id, so a keymap is already free to point different lanes at different media; it is arithmetic on planKeymap (extend KeymapOptions to take a list of (sampleId, rootMidi) and choose per lane). Research 132 estimates "even 3 per octave kills the worst repitch artifacts" of D1 (one sample stretched across ±24 semitones is the chipmunk/mud transform: duration halves/doubles with pitch, transients sharpen/smear, formants shift). Root verification closes a loop every piece of which exists (render one minted lane with renderTrackSolosCommand, re-detect with detectPitch, refuse on mismatch) — see also the separate research-132 backlog row. Pre-audition makes `--count N` + board the documented path INTO a keymap. Pre-registered blind arm: 32-38% -> >=50% pairwise over >=8 batches. Honest caveat, 142's own: research 132 ranks keymap-v2 fifth of six and its headline is "sampling per se buys nothing; production and sound-design density buy everything" — build it because sampling should not be WORSE than it has to be, not because it wins the showdown. D6 (per-lane polyphony) is DONE and is not part of this row. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| `sampler` track kind — zone table, real polyphony, round-robin, velocity layers, sustain loops, ADSR | Research 142 §4.1 v2b, size L — the endgame keymap.ts already names in its own header ("keymap-as-lanes is the v1, not the endgame"), and the same thing the "One-shot sampler instrument track kind" roadmap row describes. Notes, not hits. A zone table — (sample, rootMidi, loMidi, hiMidi, loVel, hiVel, roundRobinGroup, loopStart, loopEnd) — and a polyphonic voice pool allocating one ToneBufferSource per SOUNDING NOTE rather than a pool per lane. ADSR rather than AHD. Fixes D3 (no round-robin: every hit is the same bytes, the machine-gun effect), D4 (velocity multiplies gain and nothing else — a real instrument changes TIMBRE with velocity), D5 (no sustain loops), D7 (per-lane buffer duplication), D8 (keymap speaks hits, not notes: no note duration semantics, no glide, no per-note pitch) and D10 (AHD is correct for a drum, wrong for a pitched instrument). Round-robin, velocity layers and sustain loops belong HERE and nowhere else: each is a "which sample, and how does it sustain" question, i.e. a zone-table question, not a lane question. Trigger: after keymap-v2a has established the multi-zone baseline to beat. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| `warp complex` — grain-based time-stretch (deliberately LAST) | Research 142 §3.5 and its "what NOT to build" list, size L. `complex` is a legal enum value in the format and is treated exactly as `off` (document.ts) — settable, and a silent no-op; `markers` is a modeled type that is always []. 142 is explicit that this SOUNDS like the headline feature and is the WORST leverage on the list: an L-sized WASM dependency (signalsmith-stretch) for a problem that downbeat-aligned cutting plus tempo-conforming largely dissolves — the honest v1 answer to "this chop is at the wrong tempo" is the one `beat showdown` already uses on ref clips (conform the PROJECT to the chop's detected bpm via foldBpmToRange, and cut chops on the source's own downbeat grid so they are bar-exact at their native tempo). Recorded now for when it is built: the corpus supplies a real argument for GRAIN-based over phase-vocoder — Ableton's Texture mode is functionally a granular engine behind the warp interface (fixed grain size plus a Fluctuation randomization), and "the SAME control that does time-stretching for textural material IS the pad-from-a-one-second-sound technique," so no separate granular engine is conceptually required. Trigger: only after items above it land and a real chop still cannot be used at the project tempo. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| `beat source add` silently peak-normalizes and fades an ingested sample | Pilot finding H3 (HIGH), 2026-07-26, MEASURED: chop-source/c003.wav went in at -14.5 LUFS / peak -2.5 dBFS and came out of `beat source add` as media/chunk-c003.wav at -18.0 LUFS / peak -6.0 dBFS — scaled x0.67 with the tail faded to zero. prepCandidate runs unconditionally (scripts/source-lib.mjs ingest()) and there is no --no-prep. This directly UNDOES `beat chop`'s own headline promise ("RAW cuts: no normalize, no silence-trim — both destroy the level and timing relationships BETWEEN chops that you are listening for"), and `beat score`'s success message recommends `beat sample` and `beat source add` interchangeably when the first registers byte-identical bytes and the second does not. Worse, the transform is UNRECOVERABLE from provenance: the sidecar records sha256/duration/license and nothing about the gain change. This is the concrete, measured case for the "raw ingest path (--raw)" row above — build them together, and record the applied gain in the sidecar either way. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| `beat resample --seconds N` / a range or single-note bounce | Pilot finding M3 (MEDIUM) and the pilot's own "biggest missing capability": the intended one-command path from a synth part to a playable instrument (`resample` -> `keymap`) produces an instrument that plays your ENTIRE riff on every key, because resample bounces the whole project and there is no range render. resample's honest pointer (`use beat excerpt`) is a dead end for the common case — excerpt selects named SONG SECTIONS and a loop-mode project has none. The pilot abandoned the intended path and built a second throwaway project containing a single long note, resampled that, and cross-registered the wav with `beat sample`; nothing in the CLI suggests that detour. `beat resample <file> <track> --seconds N` (or --note <pitch>, synthesizing a one-note scratch doc) collapses it into one command. A src/ gap, not a CLI-only one — cli/render.mjs's render length is always the whole project (render.mjs:229). | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | undefined | ⬜ Not started | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| A keymapped sampler forces 12 unwanted drum-kit lanes with no way to remove them | Pilot finding M1 (MEDIUM). A keymapped sampled instrument can only live on a drums track, so `add-track playable drums` + `keymap c4..c5` leaves an 18-lane track: 6 note lanes plus the 12 default kit lanes. There is no CLI path to a clean 6-lane sampler — `beat rm-lane` does not exist, `beat lane <l> none` reverts a lane to a synth voice but keeps it, `beat drum-kit` only offers 12-lane kits, and instrument tracks require a soundfont. Two possible fixes and they are different sizes: a `beat rm-lane` verb (XS, and useful on its own — removeLane already exists in edit.ts and is reachable only from the daemon), or the real one, the `sampler` track kind (L, filed separately above). Do the XS one first. | ✅ done | ❌ missing | ✅ done | ⬜ Not started | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| keymap should warn when the sample is a long multi-note phrase | Pilot finding M4 (MEDIUM). `beat keymap` happily mapped an 8-second, 4-note riff across 6 lanes, producing an "instrument" where every key plays the whole riff. The tool already KNOWS: `beat sample-info` discloses that it analyzed only "0.015-0.436s of 8.00s", so the pitch detector saw one note of four. Cheap fix with no new machinery: when the sample is long relative to what the detector inspected (or when the onset detector gen-trim.ts already ships finds several transients), say so before minting lanes — the same fail-loudly-with-the-fix posture the low-confidence-root refusal already has. Relatedly (M5), there is no effective-value readout for lane params: `inspect` prints only non-default keys, so nothing tells you resonance defaults to 0.7 or that `length` is in SECONDS, and the pilot burned a 46-second stem render inferring that lanes truncate playback. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| `beat metrics <dir>` — a compare table over a batch | Pilot finding M6 (MEDIUM). Choosing among 4 chops required a hand-written shell loop calling `beat metrics` once per file, because the two intended picking surfaces are a blocking web UI (`beat board`, `beat rate`) and a listening exercise (`beat audition`). Since `beat chop` produces a DIRECTORY and computeBatchFeatures already measures every wav in one into the exact feature vector board displays, a `beat metrics <dir>` printing that as a table is a small wiring job over machinery that exists — and it is what an AGENT picking chops needs, as distinct from what the owner's ears need. Related smaller items from the same pilot: `beat chop` names files c001..cNNN while `beat score` accepts only 1/vN (now stated in chop's output, L2), and an unknown command dumps all 792 lines of help instead of a did-you-mean (L3). | ✅ done | ❌ missing | undefined | ⬜ Not started | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |
| A CLI error instructs the user to make an HTTP call with no CLI equivalent | Pilot finding M2 (MEDIUM). `beat keymap` on a legacy-lane drums track errors with: `track "leg" is still on the implicit 5-lane kit — call materializeLanes first (POST /lane {op:"materialize"}) to opt into per-lane editing`. "materialize" appears nowhere in `beat`'s help, so a CLI-only user is dead-ended by daemon-internal vocabulary leaking into a CLI error. Either expose the operation on the CLI (`beat lane <file> <track> --materialize`) or have keymap materialize implicitly, since it is about to declare lanes anyway. materializeLanes already exists in src/core/edit.ts; this is a surfacing gap, not a capability gap. | ✅ done | ❌ missing | ✅ done | ⬜ Not started | [`142-sampling-toolkit.md`](research/142-sampling-toolkit.md) | — |

| Rule on FEATURE_KEYS: append, fork, or freeze (131 P0 / 138 B0) | RULED AND SHIPPED 2026-07-26 (49bc7133, decisions.md D30, research/144): option (a) — append + version + snapshot. `FEATURE_KEYS_V1` is a frozen prefix, `FEATURE_SET_VERSION` is now 2 (src/metrics/features.ts:123), stale records upgrade on read, and a key-set snapshot test enforces the invariant 136 §5 and 140 §4.4 both asked for. The ruling also drew the boundary CLAUDE.md's frozen-constants rule was reaching for: engineplusProfile/surgeplusProfile pin measured TREATMENTS and stay frozen; FEATURE_KEYS is a MEASUREMENT VECTOR, and a measurement vector is protected by a version plus a snapshot, not a taboo. Appending is permitted iff FEATURE_SET_VERSION moves in the same commit, the snapshot is updated, and the accuracy numbers are re-baselined. The original ask, for the record: THE BLOCKER UNDER THE WHOLE LADDER, and it needs a ruling before it needs a build. | 33 failing recipe gates — and no gate on any REAL recipe | FILED 2026-07-26. Commit d7491ce7 built, rendered and gate-checked all 13 shipped recipes (presets/recipes.json) and recorded 77 gates pass, 33 fail, 24 pending. Its honesty claim VERIFIES — not one band was widened to make a failure go away — but its two named remedies got no row, so the 33 failures are recorded and unowned. The remedies: (1) PER-RECIPE GATE MINING (139 §5.3's cluster step) rather than looser bands — acid-303 fails sub share 0.03 vs 5..55 because the recipe itself high-passes at 150 Hz on its source's instruction while the gate was transcribed from 131's generic bassline row; the chord/pad recipes have the same shape. (2) PER-LAYER GATES BELONG IN LEAKAGE FORM — every layer gate written as a two-sided share band failed high on its solo render (mid 92.7 vs 25..75, body 96.4 vs 12..75, reese 82.6, growl/air 91-95) while every one-sided leakage bound (`bandSubPct: [0, 25]` on a mid layer) passed. That is not thirteen recipe bugs, it is a schema lesson: on a solo render a correctly-designed layer is ~100% inside its own band, which is the GOAL. Clip-level share gates stay two-sided. One finding is an ENGINE limit and should not be chased as a recipe bug: reese-bass measures -20.97 dB width against its own <=-30 gate because dotbeat has no band-split stereo or M/S crossover, so a detuned-beating Reese and a dead-mono low end cannot both be had in one voice. THE GAP THAT MAKES THIS URGENT: nothing gate-checks a real recipe on any automated run. test/recipe.test.ts is entirely synthetic (its end-to-end group runs "over a synthesized render... without needing headless Chromium"), and 3e0beef6 renamed scripts/verify-recipes.mjs -> gen-recipe-receipts.mjs EXPLICITLY because it asserts no invariant ("it produces a committed artifact, it does not assert an invariant") — so the only thing that checks all 13 recipes is formally a non-gate, is not in package.json scripts, and is not on ui/verify-manifest.mjs's roster. OWNER: whoever next touches src/recipes/. TRIGGER: before any recipe is used to generate material for a rated batch — a recipe with an unowned failing gate must not spend owner rating time (138's own design rule). | 🔶 partial | 🔶 partial | — | ⬜ Not started | [`139-recipe-library-and-layering.md`](research/139-recipe-library-and-layering.md) | — |

## Research 141-145 backlog

| Feature | Description | Core | CLI/MCP | GUI | Status | Research | Plan |

| Retarget the engine-preset ROLL PRIOR to the measured corpus (141 §7.1-7.2) | The single highest-leverage item in 141, and it is one function: `rollParams` in scripts/curate-engine-presets.mjs:116-127, verified byte-identical to the doc this pass. 141's framing is the point — "the PRIOR, not the critic, set the attack distribution", so no amount of curation fixes it downstream. Four measured deviations, all in presets/engine-curated.json (generatedAt 2026-07-24, unchanged): (1) RELEASE, the largest, 16-39x too long — lead 1,213 ms vs Surge's 31 ms (38.8x, p91), chords 862 vs 31 (27.6x, p100), bass 642 vs 39 (16.6x); 141 calls this "cheapest fix #1 — one line in the roll prior", and note presets/engine-retargeted.json (2026-07-26) did NOT fix it either, landing lead release at 1.014 s. (2) ATTACK: the prior is log-uniform 2 ms-800 ms, median 40 ms, with only 30.6% of its mass below 12.5 ms, against a corpus whose lead/pluck/bass median is 3.91 ms and where 65-68% sit exactly on Surge's floor; the ask is >=60% of mass below 12 ms for transient roles. (3) NO SECOND OSCILLATOR: the corpus runs >=2 audible oscillators in lead 54.9% / bass 56.7% / pad 56.6% / chords 85.7%, dominant form an OCTAVE SPLIT (lead 37.9%, chords 78.6%), while 180 of 181 kept presets have unisonVoices == 1 and eight param keys with no osc2Level/subLevel. (4) The prior rolls 8 of the format's 145 fields, so nothing 141 measures as load-bearing (osc2, sub, noise, unison, filter envelope, waveshaping, a second filter, effects) is ever rolled. Also here because it is the same function: the SUPERSAW prior (3-7 voices at +/-5-25 cents, reserving +/-40-60 for a "wide" variant; >=3-voice detune median 11.4 cents, IQR 6.0-20.0, and leads run NARROWEST at 10.0 cents, opposite the folk rule), and the fact that `osc2Detune` CONFLATES TWO INTENTS — factory.json leads/pads/plucks use 5-19 cents (unison) while bass uses 1,203 and keys 1,907 (octave layering), so "a generator that samples osc2Detune uniformly will produce neither". | presets/role-parameter-stats.json has no in-repo regeneration path (141 §8, 143 §8) | VERIFIED 2026-07-26: `grep -rn role-parameter-stats scripts/ python/ src/ cli/` returns ONLY CONSUMERS — scripts/curate-surge-patches-v2.mjs:193, src/taste/surgeCuration.ts (five sites), src/recipes/schema.ts:3 (the recipe library cites it as ground truth), test/surge-curation.test.ts:382. There is no writer anywhere. 143 §8 states the problem exactly: "its pipeline lives in a scratch directory outside the repo with hard-coded absolute paths. Any design that leans on it for corroboration should promote that pipeline into scripts/ or python/ first, or accept that the artifact is a one-off snapshot." The evidence behind the verdict, kept here so the next reader does not have to re-derive it: adding a production chain to the same figure and patch wins 97.6% head-to-head (engineplus over raw engine, 42 batches, all four roles), yet the reference still beats engineplus 87.3%; and a SECOND chain on a different source buys nothing (surgeplus over surge 46.2%, below chance) — so chain DEPTH is not where the gap lives, and the missing thing is a different KIND of move. Research 143 §4.4 reaches the same item from the prior-mining side, naming "a transient shaper with attack/sustain in dB" as a top entry in the demand-ranked feature backlog. TRIGGER: already fired — D30 is the trigger. | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`144-critic-instruments.md`](research/144-critic-instruments.md) | — |
| The engineplus WIDTH INVERSION — engineplus passes rolecheck LESS than raw engine (144 §3.2) | 144 §3.2's most informative single result, and a third independent arrival at the same defect: engineplus passes rolecheck 17.2% against raw engine's 32.0%, because its frozen profile widens EVERY role to -10..-12 dB and busts the role-specific width bands. 131 §7-P5 called the frozen constant "measurably wrong in BOTH directions"; 140 D9 found the same thing from the showdown side; this is the measurement showing it costs the flagship profile its own screen. Frozen-science means the fix is a NEW NAMED PROFILE, never an edit to engineplusProfile — which is what CLAUDE.md's rule actually says and what nobody has done in four sibling cases. RELATIONSHIP TO THE EXISTING ROW: "The role-true width map, in the SHOWDOWN profile (140 D9)" covers the same defect for a DIFFERENT CONSUMER (showdown.ts:230's role:'default'); engineplus itself is uncovered by it. Whoever takes either should take both — one new per-role width map serves both consumers, and splitting them is how this got missed twice already. TRIGGER: none needed; every engineplus batch run until then produces clips the project's own screen rejects more often than the unproduced control. | ❌ missing | — | — | ⬜ Not started | [`144-critic-instruments.md`](research/144-critic-instruments.md) | — |
| Two open measurement questions from 144: the 2.1-point residual, and a 36-batch-stale score log | DEFERRED WITH TRIGGERS, filed because both are traps for the next person who re-derives a number. (a) THE RESIDUAL: the shipped combined critic lands 0.774 against 131's published 0.795 (+9.7 points where 131 predicted +11.9). 144 ruled out the obvious causes rather than papering over the gap — the optimizer is not it (GD 0.759, scipy L-BFGS-B 0.774; an L2 sweep from 1 to 3000 peaks at the WEAKEST setting and 40,000 iterations makes it worse, 0.759 -> 0.715), and width collinearity is not it (dropping widthMeanDb/stereoWidthDb moves it <=0.003). Leading hypothesis is the pair population: 1,581 pairs here vs 131's 1,538, because extract.py's per-feature coverage filter was not replicated. Stated as an unreproduced 2.1-point discrepancy. TRIGGER: anyone re-deriving 131's numbers, and — mandatorily — the next FEATURE_KEYS append, since D30 requires re-baselining accuracy in the same commit. (b) THE STALE LOG: 144 §2.4 records that origin/main's copy of the score log was 36 rated batches behind the working checkout (147 vs 183 with-sources records), so "anyone re-running these against origin/main will get 0.690 -> 0.771 on a smaller sample and should not read the difference as drift." §6 asks for the divergence to be reconciled before anyone re-derives. This repo has form here — CLAUDE.md documents origin/main sitting at a stale snapshot for weeks in July. TRIGGER: before any re-derivation of a §2 accuracy number; and it is worth a one-line check in whatever script re-derives them. | — | — | — | ⬜ Not started | [`144-critic-instruments.md`](research/144-critic-instruments.md) | — |
| A SECOND owner-flagged bass pair — the grind detector is calibrated on n=1 | 144 §4.2/§6. The bass-grind screen shipped (src/metrics/screens.ts:171-214 calibration, :531-545, scoped to clips with >=85% of energy below 250 Hz) and its FALSE-POSITIVE side is genuinely measured: 0/61 in-scope reference clips, 0/24 packs, 1/301 synth clips, against 121 §3.7 verbatim at 12/61 = 19.7% and 9/24 = 37.5% packs. But its SENSITIVITY side is n=1, and three of its four clauses have margins (0.85 dB, 2.7 points, 1.4 points) sitting at or inside the render-run variance floor (1.0 dB peak-domain, 2.0 points band-share) — i.e. within noise. 144's own conclusion is the ask: "a second owner-flagged bass pair is worth more than any amount of re-tuning against this one." This is narrower than, and not covered by, the existing "Bank owner-flagged listening misses into listen-bench/" row, which is about establishing the PRACTICE; this is about one specific missing datapoint that three thresholds depend on. TRIGGER: the next time the owner flags a bass render as grindy — bank the flagged render, the fixed render, and the owner's own words IN THAT SESSION, per CLAUDE.md, because the pre-fix render is usually gone by the next one. | 🔶 partial | — | — | ⬜ Not started | [`144-critic-instruments.md`](research/144-critic-instruments.md) | — |
| MCP twins for `beat recipe` and `beat rolecheck` | FILED 2026-07-26 out of the docs sweep. The dotbeat skill's cli-reference asserted "everything else has a tool" over a CLI-only list that did not include either verb; both shipped CLI-only this week and neither is a deliberate omission the way the orchestration verbs (daemon, showdown, pilot, board) are. `beat rolecheck` in particular is the one call an MCP-driven agent most needs, because it is the call that tells the agent NOT to spend the owner's ratings on a clip — an agent that can only reach it by shelling out will mostly not reach it. Both already follow the shape CLAUDE.md requires: all the logic is in src/ (src/taste/rolecheck.ts, src/recipes/), so the twin is a schema plus a call, not a second implementation, and each gets a row in the CLI-MCP parity table test. Same pass corrected the counts these live among: 89 CLI commands (was documented as 87) and 75 MCP tools (documented as 71 in two places and "~58" in `beat help mcp`). | ✅ done | ✅ done | — | ⬜ Not started | [`144-critic-instruments.md`](research/144-critic-instruments.md) | — |

Practical posture: presets/engine-retargeted.json and surge-retargeted.json are usable; keep the D25 training holdout unchanged. (2) MINIMAX ToS read: skipped — the provider eliminated itself on reliability (9 of 10 calls failed through fal), so its rights posture is academic. Re-trigger: only if MiniMax is re-evaluated. (3) SURGE factory/third-party patch content: the owner's reasoning — these are patches authored by users for other users to MAKE MUSIC with, so using them to make music is their intended purpose. Our exposure was never music-making; it was only ever REDISTRIBUTING rendered audio, which D31's posture already prevents (renders stay eval-private and gitignored). Closed as not-a-concern-for-our-use. | Surge render-on-edit: cut the ~1.1 s edit→hear round trip | MEASURED 2026-07-27 (ui/verify-surge-gui-playback.mjs, real app, real project): 1123 ms from a note edit to hearing the new render for a 4-bar phrase, 3778 ms for a 24-bar project. The breakdown for the 4-bar case is ~810 ms inside python/surge_render.py (a fresh interpreter + surgepy instance + patch load per render), ~150 ms of daemon debounce, and ~50 ms of browser fetch+decode. DEFERRED because the mechanism is honest and usable at ~1 s and the fix is a real piece of engineering: a persistent surgepy WORKER process holding the loaded patch, so a re-render is a note-list write and a buffer read rather than a process spawn (the same worker the "Board v2 set" row already names). RE-TRIGGER, either of: the owner reports the lag breaks the edit loop in a real session, OR any surge project reaches the point where a routine edit is over ~2 s (long phrases already are — the 24-bar case is 3.8 s, so this fires the moment surge is used for a whole arrangement rather than a 4-bar figure). | ❌ missing | — | ❌ missing | ⬜ Not started | [`114-synth-engine-alternatives.md`](research/114-synth-engine-alternatives.md) | [`surge-track.md`](docs/surge-track.md) |

---

## Process

Every stream/phase that ships a feature updates `scripts/roadmap-data.mjs` (not this file
directly) as part of its own completion checklist, then re-runs the generator — don't let it
drift stale. When a stream *researches* a feature without building it, add the row the moment the
research doc lands with status "not-started" and a research link, so scoped-but-unbuilt work is
visible in the same place as everything else, not buried in `docs/research/`.

Last regenerated: 2026-07-12.
